# Project TinyWorld — Agent Context
- **Black-tree root cause + fat-blade grass LOD (Aug 12, commit a43b994, bundle tw-staging-app-HxAAvdXU.js):** KJ screenshot (leslielab prod): every tree a black skeleton + mid-distance hills bald green. **Trees:** the canopy was never "missing" — the LEAF SHADER FAILED TO LINK ("Too many attributes (aSuper)", MeshLambertMaterial) and drew black since the Aug 6 face-mask work. `aFaceMask` was declared+statically used in EVERY layer's vertex shader; a statically-used attribute counts against the GPU's 16-slot limit even with no buffer bound, and leaves already sat at exactly 16 (pos/nrm/uv + instanceMatrix×4 + aoIdx + 6×aoF + treeShade + aSuper) → 17 → link fail. Trunks = exactly 16 → still rendered (dark brown = the "burnt" look). The Aug 6 fix (70ae81c) only removed the tree-layer BUFFER, not the declaration, so it never actually fixed the pixels. Now both declaration and mask line are gated on `_hasFaceMask`. **LEAVES HAVE ZERO SPARE ATTRIBUTE SLOTS — any new per-instance data must ride in an existing vec4.** Verified headless on `w_mruu7kpe_9vwzy5` (213k saved world, loads fine): 0 shader errors (was 2), canopies pixel-verified green (Telegram). **Grass:** LOD thinned count at constant width → bald rings. Blades now widen continuously with camera distance in the vertex shader (`uLodWiden*` ramp NEAR→XFAR, default gain 2.6, `?grasswide=`); rings now 45/100/200/340 voxels at frac 1/0.42/0.14/0.05, zero only past XFAR (`?grasslodxfar=`); `__tw.grassInfo().lod` reports xfar+widen. **Also seen once on the small world: racy "Cannot access 'mi' before initialization" world-load abort (TDZ in the minified bundle) — 6 reload hunts didn't reproduce; if a world load fails with that banner, reload first.** KJ device check = leslielab trees green, meadow reads full at mid-distance, far grass fat-but-sparse, cutoff only at very long range.
- **Spring siting v11 — middling height + centroid pull (Aug 12, commit cb322c7):** KJ: "water source too high up — more central to center mass and middling in height." v10's lexicographic highest-first rank always climbed the tallest plateau. New blended score in `chooseShelf()` (`src/water-spring-runtime.ts`): `|y − targetY|/hSpan + CENTRAL_W·(centralDist/centralNorm)`, lowest wins, flatness breaks ties. `targetY = minTop + hSpan·?springheight` (default 0.55); `?springcent` central weight (default 1). Gates (flatness/thickness/drop) unchanged; persist 10→11 re-sites saved worlds once. Headless-verified on the staging debug world: sited y=44 with targetY 62.8 (v10 would have taken the highest crest) — log `[spring] plateau sited … score … (targetY …)`. **KJ device check on `w_msgc8dco_geqwkn` = middling, central spring; tune `?springheight=0.65` higher / `?springcent=0.5` less center pull.**

- **Plateau spring siting v10 + pwater domain floor (Aug 11 PM, commits 88e7b6a+aea8bf2, bundle tw-staging-app-DgT_utPL.js):** KJ (screenshot of leslielab `w_msgc8dco_geqwkn`): "water should come from plateau or relatively flat surface (higher elevation) not from the point of a wall - 2) the water height seems to have limitations". v9's shelf test (>=2 near-level 4-neighbours, score=biggest drop) was satisfied along a 1-cell wall ridge → tallest wall crest won, whole-world-height fall. Rewritten `chooseShelf()` in `src/water-spring-runtime.ts`: **two-tier plateau pass** — tier 1 = thick ground (y-1,y-2 solid) + 5x5 flatness >= `?springflat` (default 0.7, missing columns count AGAINST) + a drop >= `?springdrop` (8) within `?springdroprad` (6, Chebyshev); tier 2 (only if tier 1 empty) waives thickness but widens flatness to 7x7 — a structural roof/table slab qualifies, a lumpy tree canopy can't. Score = highest, then flattest, then central (drop no longer scores → extreme cliffs not preferred). Persist version 9->10 re-sites saved worlds once. **Height-limitation root cause** in `src/pwater/tinyworld-pwater.ts`: crop box was y0=0 (absolute), so a high-sited spring blew MAX_CELLS vertically and the while-loop squeezed XZ to a skinny column (pool starves, stream culled at box walls). Now `y0 = max(0, lowest world solid − 6)` — ny tracks terrain relief, not altitude; off-world falls drain a few cells under the island underside (open boundary) instead of plunging to absolute 0. **Headless-verified on the small debug world** (thin floating platform, YUCHI): tier 2 sited (41,9,11) = the TABLE top (only elevated flat sheet there — colYs [0,1,9]; v9's "wall-top (37,9,12)" was the same table's edge), water cascades off the table to the grass, box spans full footprint (-2..66 x -2..59). Heavy leslielab world wedges headless (known) — **KJ device check = on `w_msgc8dco_geqwkn`: spring sits on a broad flat high area (not the wall point), fall height reads sane, pool actually fills.**
- **Cliff-shelf spring siting v9 (Aug 11, commit 8267066, bundle tw-staging-app-DOCPxrpf.js) [superseded by v10 above]:** KJ: "can we make the water on one of the cliffs / shelf?" New `chooseShelf()` in `src/water-spring-runtime.ts` runs BEFORE basin/outlet: picks a high ledge — open air >=6 cells above (emitter clear), thick solid column beneath (y-1,y-2 solid, so floating canopies/roof shells can't qualify), >=2 near-level 4-neighbours (real shelf, not a spike), and a 4-neighbour column top >= `?springdrop` (default 8) cells lower; missing-neighbour void edges never count as a drop so it can't pour off the world. Best = biggest drop, then highest, then central. `?springsite=basin` restores the old order; persist version 8->9 so saved worlds re-site once (`?springresite=1` still forces). pwater side unchanged — an open-sky shelf gets the floor+2 hover (report mode "basin", roofY null); a roofed shelf would still ceiling-mount. **Headless-verified on the staging debug world:** springReport origin (37,9,12) = wall-top ledge; cellInfo confirms 3 level neighbours at y=9 + west neighbour top y=1 (8-cell interior cliff onto grass, NOT void); pwater source {mode:basin, basinY:9, roofY:null, emitY:11}, ~4.7k particles flowing; screenshot shows water pooled on the shelf at the plateau lip (fall face itself not cleanly framed headless — grass canopy + cloud-sea haze; shots on Telegram). NOTE: water-spring-runtime.ts was previously UNTRACKED in the project repo — commit 8267066 adds it. **KJ device check = fall reads off the wall-top ledge down to the grass, pool forms at the base, water doesn't sheet into the void.**
- **Waterlog hysteresis — shore flicker fix, LAB-PROVEN (Aug 11, tinyworld commit d6ad363, bundle tw-staging-app-BPeN8XCr.js; sandbox commit in water-sim-sandbox):** KJ: "grass not working fully — simulate in another environment, check water covering + flickering, fix there, then apply to staging." Built a grass-moisture LAB in `water-sim-sandbox` (`src/sim/grass-moisture.ts` + /swater page): faithful port of the staging moisture pipeline (heavy distance pass, light ease, soil memory, waterlog) driven by the shallow-water solver on SIM time, with reversal-flicker telemetry, per-column re-blade flash overlay, an `edgeNoise` knob emulating pwater's particle→cell sampling blink, sim `evap` (mass-counted), and a deterministic `__sw.advance(seconds)` driver (headless RAF throttles — never trust wall-clock runs). **Diagnosis:** soil memory (Aug 9) is SOLID — zero irrigation/tint churn under slosh+noise. The flicker is entirely the WATERLOG path: instant tuft→blades recovery + submersion-timer reset on a single dry read → 88 un-logs + 48 re-logs per 50s slosh A/B. **Fix (both repos): symmetric hysteresis** — a dry read within `WATERLOG_GAP_MS` (default 2×IRRIG_MS=16s, `?wlgap`) does NOT break a submersion streak; a waterlogged column recovers only after `WATERLOG_DRY_MS` (default = hold 60s, `?wldry`) of continuous dryness; stale-stamp cleanup + prune. Same A/B after: 8 un-logs. `__tw.waterlogReport()` now shows gapMs/dryMs/tracked. Full lifecycle verified in the lab: flood → cover → waterlog; recede (evap) → recover ~dryMs later → soil hold → fade → settle, flicker 0. Build+restart+errors clean, route chunk imports the new bundle. **KJ device check = staging shore under the spring pool: tufts/blades stop popping while the pool sloshes; grass still recovers ~1 min after true recession.**
- **Ceiling-mounted spring source (Aug 11, commit fc3fdb6, bundle tw-staging-app-BG2bHwj2.js):** KJ: "water source need to be set to high location but on ceiling" (+ "water not pooling correctly"). In `src/pwater/tinyworld-pwater.ts` the emitter no longer hovers at basin+2: it hangs 1.7 cells under the LOWEST solid roof found in the 3×3 columns over the sited basin (headroom ≥ 6 cells required; falls back to basin+2 when open-air). Crop box y1 extended to cover the roof (`max(origin.y+10, emitY+4)`). Side benefit for the pooling complaint: injection at the pool surface shoved particles sideways over the lip (the sandbox "balls pushed out" behavior); a high mouth stays clear of the back-pressure gate and the pool fills by fall-in. **KJ device check = fall reads from the roof, pool actually fills/pools in the pit.**
- **Hi-fi water surface is the pwater DEFAULT (Aug 10, commits 1545efd/…, bundle tw-staging-app-BC5Q5r0G.js+):** KJ approved the water-sim-sandbox /swater "Hi-Fi" look ("apply to staging now"). New `src/pwater/fluid-hifi.ts` + mode switch in `tinyworld-pwater.ts`: particles are binned per XZ column (binFields: top/min height, mean flow, speed, downward-impact, foam density; the relaxSurface blur provides the height field) into two bilinear float DataTextures; a static subdivided grid (2 verts/cell, cell space, group scaled by voxel) displaces by the height field. Fragment: scene-prepass refraction (own sceneRT + DepthTexture), Beer-Lambert by true view thickness, 16-step SSR, slope-detected translucent waterfall curtains, shoreline/impact foam, sim-impact mist Points. Presentation = scene→sceneRT, blit, water+mist over it (shader-side occlusion vs scene depth) — FEWER passes than SSFR (which stays available via `?pwrender=ssfr`). Composer bypass while pwater presents is UNCHANGED. Live knobs: `__tw.pwater({absorb,refract,rippleAmp,flowAdv,ssr})`; report shows `renderMode`/`hifi`. **Two bugs found in headless verification (fixed): (1) autoClear wiped the scene blit — the two-render canvas present needs manual clear discipline (save/restore autoClear + clear color); (2) foam breakup noise used flow×absolute-uTime advection which decorrelates to per-pixel salt noise as the page ages — now bounded two-phase advection (ripple()); same fix applied to the sandbox. Plus safe-normalize on the flow dir (NaN guard).** Pixel-verified on the staging debug world headless: world composites through the hi-fi present, spring pit shows coherent whitewater, errors clean. **KJ device check = look/motion/framerate, esp. bigger pools/rivers (?pwscale=1.4 flood).** Camera-finding note: debug world voxel=0.0225 (`__tw.state()`), spring pit ≈ render (0.05, 2.2–2.6, 0.63).
- **Grass soil memory — green persists after water recedes (Aug 9 PM, commit 75bda68, bundle tw-staging-app-Bomq7Muj.js):** KJ: "grass green calculation too sudden — once green should stay that way for much longer, and not recalculate all the time when the water recedes." Root cause: water-proximity moisture had NO memory (unlike rain's 2-day GREEN_HOLD) — receding/sloshing water flipped `distDry` targets and the shore yellowed in 22s + re-tinted/re-bladed every heavy pass. Fix in the moisture model (`tw-staging-app.tsx` ~20074): (1) **soilWetMs map** — a column with waterMoist ≥ 0.5 stamps its last-wet wall-clock time; after recession it holds soilMoist=1 for `SOIL_HOLD_MS` (default 4h, `?soilholdh`/`?soilhold`), then ramps to 0 over `SOIL_FADE_MS` (10min, `?soilfade`); target = 1 − max(waterMoist, rainMoist, soilMoist), so the ease target never flips during the hold → zero shore churn. (2) **Irrigation membership held by the same memory** — a column leaving the dilated set keeps green blades while soil is wet. (3) `EASE_DRY_MS` default 22s → 60s (`?decayms`). In-session only (map not persisted — reload after recession seeds columns at distance dryness; persistence = follow-up if KJ wants). Prune with distDry. Diagnostics: `__tw.aridReport()` → soilWet/soilHoldMs/soilFadeMs/dryEaseMs. Build+restart+errors clean. **No pixel proof (headless wedge): KJ device check = shore stays green as the pool evaporates/sloshes, slow fade after ~4h.**
- **Laser origin at barrel tip + water surface relaxation (Aug 9 PM, commit 2d99f32, staging bundle tw-staging-app-CTmisxsD.js):** (1) KJ: "laser start point needs to be at tip of canon, not inside canon" — the aim-mode beam started at the RightHand BONE origin (inside the cannon housing). Now the barrel length is measured at Sentinel load (max projection of verts skinned ≥0.5 to RightForeArm/RightHand onto the cannon axis past the hand bone — rigid barrel, pose-invariant; logged as "[sentinel] barrel tip measured: N") and `beamStart = muzzle bone + tipLen × beamDirection` (collinear ⇒ aim/carve unchanged, only the hidden segment is trimmed). Live override `__twAimBox.tip` (world units, −1 = measured, clamp ≤ voxel·30); `__twSentinelArm.tipLen` reports the measurement. (2) KJ: "smooth out the tops of water surfaces? averaging out?" — render-side surface relaxation in `src/pwater/tinyworld-pwater.ts` (`relaxSurface`): per frame, per-XZ-cell top-particle heights → separable box blur over WATER bins only (shores never average against dry land) → slow near-surface particles (top 1.3·cellD layer, speed-gated to 0 at ≥3 cell/s) blend rendered y toward the local surface. **SIM UNTOUCHED** (doctrine-safe: no forced lake flattening in physics — this is renderer-side reconstruction of the surface the particles already imply, like the SSFR screen-space smoothing but world-space and view-independent). Defaults ON: `?pwrelax=0.85` amount (0 = off), `?pwrelaxr=2` blur radius in cells; live `pwater({relax, relaxRad})`, values in the pwater report settings. **No pixel proof (headless world-load wedge persists): KJ device check = still-pool tops read as one sheet (his ?pwscale=1.4 test), rivers/falls keep shape, laser starts at the muzzle opening.** NOTE: commit 2d99f32 also swept up ~500 lines of previously-uncommitted-but-shipped aim/deadzone work already live in the Space copy.

  - **Aim parallax fix — cannon now aims at the REAL reticle hit (Aug 9 PM):** KJ: "the sentinel aiming is not where the reticle is pointing." Root cause: `aimSentinelCannon` aimed the muzzle at a FIXED-distance virtual point `aimAnchor + aimDir×6H` on the camera centre ray. The muzzle is offset from the lens, so muzzle→(near 6H point) diverges from the camera ray by parallax and the arm/beam landed off the reticle — while the carve target (`chargeState` via `marchRay` from screen centre) was correct, so beam ≠ block ≠ reticle. Fix: `marchRay(origin?, direction?)` now takes an optional ray and returns the entry-face world point (`hitX/Y/Z`, `dist`); `aimSentinelCannon` marches the cached lens ray (`aimAnchor/aimDir`) and sets `aimTarget` = the real hit point (fallback = far point along the ray for sky aim). Both the camera centre ray and the muzzle ray now converge on the SAME real point (the block being carved), so arm + beam + reticle agree at every distance, not just where a block happens to sit at 6H. Build+restart clean, errors clean. **No pixel proof (headless world-load still wedged) — KJ device check on staging = arm/beam land on the reticle at near AND far walls.**

  - **Spartan-laser carve phases (Aug 9, same session, KJ approved the Halo-style design):** Sentinel-large machine beam only (drone beam untouched; its base material look is re-set each frame). Phase machine in `updateMachineBeam` (`machineBeamPhase`: off/charge/carve/collapse, live at `window.__twBeamPhase`): **hold → CHARGE** — thin beam with irregular stutter (new pseudo-random value every 45ms) riding a growing envelope over the existing 850ms `chargeState`, NO carving; **first charge completes → CARVE** — `completePickup` machine path sets phase=carve; sustained WIDE beam (outer 0.34→0.95 voxel, core 0.10→0.28, impact 1.2×) that stays lit through the 90ms recharge gap + later charges via the `lastBeamSnap` cache (capsule ticks keep firing at the old ~940ms cadence — carve economy unchanged); target lost >260ms (sky aim) → collapse; **release → COLLAPSE** — 320ms replay of the cached beam under a decaying 110ms square-wave stutter (2–3 fast blinks; slow-build/fast-die asymmetry). All release paths funnel through ONE central transition on `!beamHeld` in updateMachineBeam (no per-call-site hooks). **Tap-zap:** release BEFORE the charge completes carves exactly the aimed block (`tapZapBlock`: hopper-capacity checked, `removeResolvedBlock` + hopper accounting like the capsule, pick/debris fx, settle+support) — wired into canvas mouseup + `machineBeamInputRef.stop` (mobile); window blur/off-canvas release cancels WITHOUT zapping. In `tw-staging-app.tsx` the enabled expr is now `raygi.supported && _raygiFlag === "1"` — RayGI only runs with `?raygi=1` or the live debug toggle `__tw.raygi({enabled:true})` (volume build is skipped at boot when off). Interiors will get explicit placed lights when rooms are built (NOT RayGI). Built + restarted; served in `tw-staging-app-Ba9hwuBe.js` on both `/tinyworld-staging` and prod `/tinyworld` (same shell app). Feel = KJ device check.

## Lighting-fidelity sequence (Jul 24)

KJ ruling: TinyWorld fidelity work should concentrate on lighting and shadow behavior: direct/key lighting, cast and contact shadows, bounce lighting, bounce-colored shadow/fill, ambient occlusion, and emissive light interaction. Prefer techniques that reveal form and material depth. **Never invent an interior light merely because the player is covered or indoors.** Light must originate from an actual source in the world—sun, moon, an emissive object, or a placed/built light—and bounce lighting must be derived from those real sources and nearby material colors. Geometry may occlude or attenuate existing light; it must not trigger an artificial ambient lamp. Avoid bloom, grading, exposure shifts, warm washes, or other post-processing as primary fidelity improvements when they flatten or recolor the scene.

- **AO** was already in: wall/ground greedy meshers bake per-vertex AO; instanced layers use corner-vertex "Project-Gaia" AO. A parallel session owns greedy chunk-boundary seam debugging (`?diag=` harness) — coordinate before touching `greedy.ts` / `wall-greedy.ts` / `ground-greedy.ts`.
- **Artificial interior lighting rejected (Jul 25):** the covered-pocket detector produced a delayed yellow wash by inventing warm ambient illumination without an in-world source. KJ rejected the model, not just the color. It is disabled by default; future interior rendering may occlude real outdoor light and calculate bounce from real sources, but must not create an ambient lamp merely because the player is covered. The legacy diagnostic remains opt-in with `?interior=1`; debug: `window.__twInterior`.
- **Emissive-casts-light**: the promoted super tree's canopy centroid gets a real PointLight (`superGlowLight`, green 0x35c96e, peak 2.0, distance `span*0.3`, decay 2, castShadow OFF for iOS). Ramps with daylight in the tint loop; hard-flips on the leaf-glow edge under `?tod=` overrides. Debug: `window.__twSuperLight`. Verified by pixel-diff A/B (light off vs boosted) — illumination lands on neighboring buildings; ground-level feel needs device check.
- Next experiments should stay inside the lighting/shadow doctrine: bounce-colored hemisphere ground tint, screen-space or geometry-aware contact/bounce shadows, secondary light from nearby surfaces, emissive scatter light pools, and stable shadow coverage around the player. Evaluate each with A/B rendered pixels and preserve the base palette.
- **Jul 25 lighting-fidelity pass (SHIPPED to the shared staging+prod app):** four changes in `tw-staging-app.tsx` (project copy synced). (1) **Bounce rig** — the dormant hemi + `under` lights are now DERIVED bounce: hemi sky half = live sky palette, ground half = world ground albedo (PAL grass/dirt/dryGrass mix, snow-lerped in winter) × the active key light; `under` = the same bounce as an uplight (keyI × 0.16); `fill` color tracks the sky palette instead of fixed navy. Ramps with the real sun/moon (moon bounce ×0.5). Kill `?bounce=0`, live `__tw.bounce({hemi,under,moonFrac,enabled})`, state `__twBounce`. (2) **GTAO** replaces the disabled kernel SSAOPass (esm.sh three@0.165 `GTAOPass`): world-space radius `max(0.12, voxel*8)`, thickness `voxel*1.5`, 12 samples, Poisson denoise, blendIntensity 0.55, composer path only (mobile-walk direct render unaffected). Default ON; kill `?ssao=0`, live `__tw.setSSAO` / `__tw.gtao({blend,ao,denoise,output})`. (3) **Emissive canopy pool** — every leaf cluster ≥120 leaves registers its centroid (`glowTreeCells`, harvested in the super-canopy union-find); a 2-light budgeted PointLight pool can attach to the canopies nearest the player at night (castShadow off). **KJ Jul 25 (same day): "get rid of the emissive tree lights - does not look good yet" → BOTH defaults OFF** — `SUPER_GLOW_PEAK = 0` (the 1.15 retry after the zeroed 2.0 was also rejected) and the pool is opt-in via `?glowlight=1`. Mechanism retained for a future look pass: `__tw.glowLights({enabled,count,peak,super})`, state `__twGlowPool`. Emissive-casts-light needs a redesigned look (not just intensity) before another default-on attempt. (4) **Shadow follow-box deadband** — sun/moon shadow boxes commit a texel-snapped center and hold it until the focus drifts >30% of the box half or the light direction/box size changes (stable-CSM discipline: bit-identical shadow camera while roaming inside the band, repaints only at commit edges). Live `__tw.shadowDeadband(frac)`, 0 = old per-frame recentering. **Verified:** Space build passed, `get_space_errors` clean, debug world built full-pipeline headless with the new rig live (hemi 0.92 / under 0.48 at midday, GTAO on), day A/B pixel-diff RMSE 2.6% with visible underside bounce lift. **Pending KJ device check:** day warmth tuning, GTAO up close, night glow pool (debug world has NO trees — needs a treed world), deadband feel while walking. Zo Browser wedged in world loading again (~4.5 min at "loading debug scan"); live checks used the localhost harness.

- **RayGI hybrid ray-traced lighting v1 (Jul 25, staging default):** `src/tw-raygi.ts` + 6 integration points in `tw-staging-app.tsx` (project copies synced). Secondary Amanatides–Woo DDA ray pass over a downsampled RGBA8 occupancy+albedo volume in a **3D texture** (K=2 grid voxels/cell, ≤176³; debug world: dims 110×96×147, 52k solid cells, 6.2MB). Per pixel at half-res-per-axis: 1 jittered sun-visibility ray (cone aperture → soft penumbra after temporal) + 2 cosine-hemisphere bounce rays whose hit cells are lit by a real secondary sun-shadow trace — every photon sources from the live sun/moon key + hemi sky palette, no invented ambient (doctrine-compliant). Temporal accumulation with reprojection (blend 0.88). Composited in the composer AFTER GTAO / BEFORE bloom: scene × soft ray-shadow (uShadowI 0.35) + bounce (uBounceI 0.55, scene-color-modulated). Raster path 100% untouched; mobile-walk direct render never runs it. Volume built at world-ready from the FINAL layer buffers (water excluded — transmits light); edits stream through count-based `raygiEditHooks` in `removeBlockFrom`/`placeAt` (throttled 400ms full-texture re-upload); other add paths (worker builds) are stale until `__twRayGI.rebuild()`. **Defaults (formalized Jul 25, KJ: "formalize them into staging and production"): ON for desktop on BOTH `/tinyworld-staging` and `/tinyworld` prod; OFF on mobile.** (The original staging-only gate `pathname.includes("tinyworld-staging")` was removed — the gate is now just `!isMobile`.) Force `?raygi=1`, instant kill `?raygi=0`, live `__tw.raygi({enabled,bounce,shadow,aperture,blend,maxDist,div})`, state `window.__twRayGI`. Prepass reuses the outline normal/depth target (full scene re-render with MeshNormalMaterial when GI on — the main known cost). Known v1 limits: Sentinel/Drone GLBs not in the volume (machines cast no ray shadow), no emissive cells yet (shader path exists — future lever for the rejected tree-glow look), bilinear upsample can halo thin edges. Build passed, `get_space_errors` clean, volume-build log verified live headless; **look A/B + walk feel = KJ device check.**
- **RayGI v1.2 — per-surface bounce strength + reach (Jul 25, KJ: "every surface should have a bounce amount… bounce strength influences the bounce light distance — it's not the sun which is uniform"):** `RAYGI_BOUNCE_STRENGTH` table in `tw-raygi.ts` (snow 0.9, wall/ceiling 0.7, leaves 0.4, grass/dryGrass 0.35, trunks/dirt/hidden_dirt 0.3, wet 0.15) is baked into the volume ALPHA channel at build and on `addVoxel` edits: empty=0, solid=0.45–1.0 encoding strength 0–1 (`encodeStrength`), so the DDA solid test (a>0.4) is unchanged. In the bounce loop the HIT cell's strength scales BOTH radiance and reach: `reach = mix(uReachMin, uReachMax, str)`, falloff `1−smoothstep(0.3·reach, reach, bt)` — snow carries across a courtyard, dark asphalt dies in a few cells. New params `reachMin`/`reachMax` (set vs cell at build: cell×10 / =maxDist) are live via `__tw.raygi({reachMin,reachMax})`; ALSO fixed: `maxDist`/`sunMaxDist` knob writes were dead after build (uniforms only set at buildVolume) — `update()` now syncs all distance uniforms per frame. Rebalanced defaults for the weaker per-material fill: `bounce 0.55→1.5`, `shadow 0.5→0.42`, `giScale 0.45→0.6`. **Verified on the Mac testbed (pixel-aligned live-toggle A/B):** shadow=0 isolation → lit px +0.8 / dark px +10.6 (bounce lifts only shadows, zero wash); shipped defaults → chroma preserved (50.2 vs 49.7), dark px +6.9, lit-area deepening is the soft ray-shadow feature (walls/alcoves get real graded shading; frame reads deeper, NOT muddier — visually confirmed both frames, Sentinel visible). Frames on Telegram. NOTE for future A/Bs: the embodied Sentinel idles/wanders — capture on/off within ~1s of each other or framing noise swamps the metrics. Look/walk feel = KJ device check.
- **RayGI v1.1 — wash fix (Jul 25, after KJ's A/B screenshots):** KJ compared GI on/off and flagged that v1 "bounces everything" — the GI frame was globally lifted, pale, desaturated. Three causes, all fixed in `tw-raygi.ts` + `tw-staging-app.tsx` (project copies synced): (1) **miss rays now contribute NOTHING** — a bounce ray that exits to open sky previously added `uSkyCol`, so every open-air pixel got a sky-colored additive lift; sky ambient already lives in the raster hemi rig, so this was double-counted light. (2) **Hemi rig scales down while GI composites** — new `BOUNCE.giScale` (0.45, tunable via `__tw.bounce({giScale})`) multiplies hemi+under intensity when `raygiState.enabled && raygi.ready()`, so exactly one system owns sky/ground bounce; kills the double-count, restores automatically when GI is off (tint loop reads it live). (3) **Bounce is occlusion-weighted fill, not a blanket add** — composite now `scene × mix(1, 0.45+0.55·sunVis, uShadowI=0.5)` (deeper ray shadow, was 0.6+0.4/0.35) and bounce is multiplied by `fill = mix(1.0, 0.25, sunVis)`: ray-shadowed pixels get full bounce, sunlit ground only 25%. **Verified on Mac Chrome, pixel-aligned live-toggle A/B (same camera/world):** RMSE 2.55% (GI still does real work), GI-on mean brightness now ≤ baseline (163.5 vs 164.7 — wash gone), chroma preserved (23.7 both), delta concentrated in dark pixels (−2.4) vs lit (−0.1). Frames on Telegram. Close-up walk feel = KJ device check. Also fixed: `window.__tw` was WHOLLY reassigned at two scene-build sites (`{ camera, orbit }` and the big cameraOrbit literal), clobbering earlier-registered knobs — `__tw.raygi` was unreachable after world load. Both sites now spread-merge the prior `__tw`; knob verified live post-world-ready. Any future `(window).__tw = {...}` must spread-merge, never replace.
- **PAUSED then FORMALIZED Jul 25 — KJ: "all the changes we've made so far are good" → "formalize them into staging and production."** RayGI v1.2 + unified Drone chase + testbed are the approved shipped state, and the RayGI desktop default was promoted to production `/tinyworld` (see the defaults note in the v1 entry above). Build passed, bundle verified (minified gate reads `!isMobile` with no staging-path check; `/tinyworld` route chunk imports the new app chunk), `get_space_errors` clean, AND runtime-confirmed in real Chrome on the Mac at prod `https://kj.zo.space/tinyworld?debugWorld=1` with no `?raygi` param: `worldReady:true, raygiEnabled:true, giReady:true, vol 110×96×147, bounce 1.5, shadow 0.42`. Parked next levers (not started): (1) Sentinel/Drone GLBs into the GI volume or otherwise grounding them — KJ's phone shots show the Sentinel reads as floating because its raster shadow doesn't land visibly on instanced grass; investigation of whether the custom grass material samples the shadow map was mid-flight when paused. (2) Grass highlight tint reads slightly acid-yellow in foreground at midday — check palette/tint, not exposure. (3) Wet-surface specular ray (mirror bounce for water/wet cells) — alpha channel already has headroom for the flag.

- **RayGI 2-bounce experimental + GTAO/GI ladder A/B (Aug 7):** `?gibounces=2` / `__tw.raygi({bounces:2})` adds a second indirect hop in `tw-raygi.ts` (uBounces uniform, synced per-frame in update(); sky/miss still contributes nothing). Five-state ladder captured headless at a fixed debug-world pose (`debug-shots/lighting/ladder/`, montage on Telegram): GTAO −0.1 mean in the shadow crop, RayGI ray-shadow −1.5, 1-hop bounce +2.4; **2-hop ≈ 1-hop outdoors (within animation noise)** — confirms the one-bounce-is-enough call; keep 1 as default, radiance cache stays the shortlisted interior lever. **CRITICAL A/B GOTCHA (cost one wasted ladder): while particle water presents (pwCtrl active), the composer is bypassed (documented v1 tradeoff, tw-staging-app.tsx ~21044) — ALL GTAO/RayGI/bloom knobs silently no-op on-screen (state mutates, pixels don't; pass uniforms/enabled go stale at their last composer-frame values). Any lighting A/B on a watery world MUST load `?pwater=0` first.** Also: voxel clouds drift into fixed-pose framings mid-ladder — hide via `__twClouds.mesh` visible=false for capture sessions; and 2-bounce at full res can wedge the SwiftShader headless tab (bake it via URL param on a fresh load instead of a live knob).

- **Ambient OFF on desktop + GI up (Jul 31, SHIPPED staging+prod, commit 1cb6e3d):** KJ ruling after a debug-world A/B — the flat unoccluded `AmbientLight` was the biggest cast-shadow lifter (washed shadows pale/flat), and it's the least doctrine-defensible fill (a directionless lamp). On DESKTOP, RayGI + the occluded hemi rig now carry the shadow fill, so the flat lamp is redundant there. Two default changes in `tw-staging-app.tsx` (project + Space synced): (1) `AMB_SCALE` default `1 → isMobileRef.current ? 1 : 0` — **ambient off on desktop, unchanged on mobile** (mobile has no RayGI to refill → would crush to black). (2) RayGI `bounce` default `1.5 → 1.9` (desktop-only; GI dialed up "a bit" to carry the deeper shadows). Both stay URL-overridable: `?amb=1` restores desktop ambient, `?gibounce=N` / `?gishadow=N` tune GI. **Verified headless (agent-browser on localhost:3099 — capture WORKS on the debug world now: drive `__tw.camera`/`orbit` in, `agent-browser eval`/`screenshot` with stdout suppressed):** no-param desktop load reads `raygiBounce:1.9`, and the close ruin frame shows deep graded interior shadow matching the approved A/B (vs the pale washed baseline). Frames in `debug-shots/lighting/` (day-ab, after-close-dusk, baked-noparams-day). Build 10s, `get_space_errors` clean, restart recovered (~2min). Ships to BOTH `/tinyworld-staging` and `/tinyworld` prod (shared bundle, no staging gate). Walk feel on device = KJ. NOTE: `?shadowfill=`/`?amb=`/`?fill=` master fill knobs (added same session) work on mobile too (scale raster fill, unlike RayGI).
  - **Follow-up: ambient OFF extended to MOBILE too (Jul 31, commit 7897dd0, KJ "same treatment on mobile"):** `AMB_SCALE` default `isMobileRef.current ? 1 : 0` → **`0` everywhere**. Empirically checked via a `?mobile=1` headless A/B (forces `isMobileRef`; mobile OVERVIEW = composer path, `walking=false` → GTAO ON, RayGI OFF): ambient-off deepens interior recesses and does NOT crush to black — the full-strength hemi/under derived-bounce rig (RayGI's `giScale` reduction doesn't apply on mobile) + baked AO + GTAO carry the fill. Corrects my earlier "mobile would crush to black" caution (wrong). **Open caveat:** the mobile WALK path is `_isMobileWalkRender` (`isMobile && walking`) → direct `renderer.render` with NO composer (no GTAO), so recesses lean on hemi/under + baked AO + shadow map only — can't verify headless, needs KJ on-device walk check. Instant revert `?amb=1`. Mobile A/B + no-param verification frames in `debug-shots/lighting/` (mobile-ab, mobile-baked-noparam).
  - **MOBILE MATCHES DESKTOP pipeline, lower res (Jul 31, commit df7574a, STAGING-gated, KJ "mobile needs to match desktop — just resolution lower"):** unifies mobile onto the full desktop composer path instead of its reduced path. Three divergences removed, all gated behind `_mobileMatchDesktop` (default `= _isStaging`, i.e. `pathname.includes("tinyworld-staging")`): (1) RayGI enabled-default `!isMobile` → `!isMobile || _mobileMatchDesktop` (RayGI now runs on mobile in match mode); (2) `_isMobileWalkRender` (`isMobile && walking` → direct render, no composer) now also requires `!_mobileMatchDesktop || ?mobilefast=1`, so mobile WALK routes through the composer (RayGI/GTAO/bloom run while walking, matching desktop); (3) `renderer.setPixelRatio(1)` → `isMobile && _mobileMatchDesktop ? _mobileRes : 1` (`_mobileRes` default 0.67, `?mobileres=N`) — lower internal res pays for the pipeline; composer + normalTarget read `getPixelRatio()` so it cascades. **STAGING-ONLY by default on purpose:** the full pipeline (RayGI 3D-texture volume + temporal history + normal prepass re-render + GTAO + bloom) risks iOS GPU/VRAM OOM ("A problem repeatedly occurred") that CANNOT be verified on headless SwiftShader (desktop GL). Prod `/tinyworld` mobile keeps the safe reduced path until KJ confirms staging doesn't crash on his iPhone, then promote (flip default or set `?mobilematch=1`). **Headless verified (`?mobile=1` on staging → match mode):** `raygiEnabled:true, giReady:true, bounce:1.9`, canvas buffer 857 / CSS 1280 = 0.67×, interior GI shadows match the desktop deep-GI look at lower res (`debug-shots/lighting/mobile-match-desktop.png`). Overrides: `?mobilematch=1/0` force, `?mobileres=N` scale, `?mobilefast=1` restore direct-render walk, `?raygi=0` kill GI. **NOT verified: iOS on-device (crash risk + walk-path feel) = KJ device check on staging.** Prepass geometry cost is res-independent, so "just lower res" pays for most-but-not-all of it — a heavy world could still OOM iOS; further mobile perf work deferred per fidelity-first ruling.
    - **REVERTED to opt-in — mobile-match OOM'd KJ's iPhone (Jul 31, commit 19d49e6):** the device check failed. On staging default-ON, KJ's iPhone rendered one frame then LOST the WebGL context — the device-memory handler (`tw-staging-app.tsx:2582`, "3D context lost (device memory) — reload to restore") fired, black canvas under intact UI, worst while WALKING (which routed through the heavy composer). Confirms the predicted iOS VRAM OOM. `_mobileMatchDesktop` default changed `_isStaging → false` (now `__diagParams.get("mobilematch") === "1"` only; the `==="0"` branch and `_isStaging` var removed). **Mobile-match is opt-in ONLY now (`?mobilematch=1`) on both staging + prod — DO NOT re-promote.** "Match desktop, just lower res" is NOT achievable on iOS Safari: the big VRAM consumers (RayGI 3D volume, normal prepass, temporal history, GTAO/bloom targets) are resolution-independent. Default mobile is back on the known-safe reduced path (RayGI off, direct-render walk, pixelRatio 1) that worked for weeks. Desktop unaffected. If mobile fidelity is revisited, it needs selective/lighter techniques (e.g. GTAO-only, no RayGI volume), not the full stack. Build clean, errors clean, restart recovered.

- **Water surface rendering v1 (Jul 25, KJ: "let's render the water differently… especially because you can't move or pick up water"):** the design unlock is that water is never a pick-up/move target, so it doesn't need to render as independent editable cubes — it can read as a liquid surface. **Scope: pure rendering layer, zero data change.** The stacked water cubes remain the authoritative structure (`settleWaterNear` owns the scan-water slotMap/instanceMatrix; the spring owns its reservoir; RayGI still EXCLUDES water — transmits light). **Shared shader in a new module `src/water-surface-shader.ts` → `installWaterSurface(THREE, mat, {waterTopY, sunDir})`** (project mirror + live Space copy identical), applied to BOTH water paths so all water reads the same: (a) scan/saved water via `addLayer(layers.water)` in `tw-staging-app.tsx`, and (b) runtime **spring** still-lake water in `water-spring-runtime.ts` (the hydro bed's actual water — starts with an EMPTY water buffer and fills bottom-up via the spring, so the addLayer path alone would NOT touch it; the spring's waterfall/drop meshes intentionally keep the plain translucent look = turbulent streams, not a calm surface). Effects on the existing `MeshStandardMaterial` (`customProgramCacheKey "tinyworldWaterSurfaceV1"`): (1) **surface emphasis** — top faces (`vWaterFaceN.y`, world face normal via `mat3(instanceMatrix)*normal`) stay crisp/reflective; side faces drop to ~0.55 alpha so the stack reads as a surface with a translucent body, not a solid blue box; (2) **animated ripple normals** — two crossing cosine wave trains over world XZ perturb the shading normal on the top face (world→view via `viewMatrix`, since `normalMatrix` is NOT in three's fragment prefix), driven by `uTime`; (3) **moving sun specular** — tight pow-60 highlight using the sun dir (`uSunDir`, world→view transformed), slides with the ripples; (4) **depth tint** — deeper water (lower world Y vs the surface `uWaterTopY`) reads darker/bluer, shallows stay clear. Fresnel-alpha (grazing→opaque) retained. Drivers: scan water — `uTime`/`uSunDir` in `__twFrame` (live sun) over `waterMats[]` next to `leafGlowMats`, `uWaterTopY` baked at build (max water Y × voxel); spring water — `uTime` + a 1s-cadence `uWaterTopY` (highest filled cell) in the spring's `tick()`, `uSunDir` left at the default overhead sun (threading live sun into tick deferred). Kill = revert; both call sites are ~2 lines around `installWaterSurface`. Build passed (5.26s), `get_space_errors` clean. Verify a world WITH water via `?terrain=hydro` (the synthetic hydrology bed) — plain debug scan is a dry interior; give the spring ~60–90s to fill a visible lake. **Next levers (not started):** shoreline foam, live sun into the spring specular, and the wet/mirror-bounce RayGI ray (alpha headroom already reserved). Look/animation/feel = KJ device check.
- **Water surface v2 — "make the water look better" (Jul 25):** enriched the shared shader (`src/water-surface-shader.ts`, `customProgramCacheKey` bumped V1→V2; project mirror + live copy identical) to read reflective/alive instead of matte blue, staying doctrine-compliant (all light sourced from the real sun + sky). Four changes: (1) **sky reflection** — new `uSkyCol` uniform; top faces add `uSkyCol × fresnel × topness × 0.75` so the surface reflects the live sky at grazing angles (this is the biggest single "reads like water" lift). Driven per-frame in `__twFrame` water loop from `hemi.color × intensity` (live sky-half palette) for scan water; spring water uses the sensible default `Color(0.32,0.46,0.66)`. (2) **dual-lobe sun specular** — replaced the single pow-60 with a broad sheen (pow-22 × 0.22, wet gloss) + tight sparkle (pow-130 × 0.9, moving glint); reads wet not plastic. (3) **finer ripple octave** — added a third higher-freq/faster cosine train to `_rx`/`_rz` (×0.28) and dialed base perturb 0.30→0.26 for sparkle without muddying. (4) **richer depth tint** — deep water pulls to saturated teal `×(0.30,0.55,0.72)`, shallows brighten toward `×(0.62,0.86,0.98)`, so the pool has body. Fresnel-alpha + surface-emphasis (sides 0.55 alpha) retained. Kill = revert the module + the one `uSkyCol` driver line in `__twFrame`. Build passed (5.16s), `get_space_errors` clean, V2 shader + `uSkyCol` verified in the live bundle. Verify a lake via `?debugWorld=1&terrain=hydro&springcap=50000` (staging needs `debugWorld=1` to auto-load the ephemeral hydro bed; give the spring ~90s to fill). Look/feel = KJ device check.

- **Scan water removed — default OFF (Jul 25, KJ: "get rid of scan water — it will disappear with evaporation anyways"):** the `addLayer(layers.water, …)` seeding in `tw-staging-app.tsx` (was at line 5015) is now gated behind `?scanwater=1` (default off). Rationale: a puddle captured in a LiDAR scan is an ephemeral snapshot, not a real source; real water comes from the rain-fed spring (a genuine in-world source), which is UNTOUCHED. Downstream degrades cleanly with no scan-water mesh: `settleWaterNear` no-ops (finds no `layer==="water"` mesh), scan-pond irrigation/waterlogging drop out (the spring still feeds both via `springCtrl.waterCells()` at ~18076), `snapshotLiveLayers` won't persist it (so it's gone on next save = "evaporated"), RayGI already excluded it. NOT changed: `aoNeighbors` still lists `layers.water` for AO baking, so a formerly-watery scan may keep faint AO darkening where the pond sat (negligible; revisit if seams show). The water-surface shader (`installWaterSurface`) still applies to the spring lake. Only affects worlds that had non-empty scan water — the `?terrain=hydro` bed uses spring water (empty scan buffer), so it's unaffected. Build passed (5.22s), `get_space_errors` clean, gate verified in the live bundle. Confirming a formerly-watery scan now shows no baked pond = KJ device check (no such test world handy here).

- **Scan water → dry divots/channels (Jul 25, KJ: "repopulate scans so they don't include water — instead have divots and channels — they could temporarily be filled with water during rain"):** build-time pass in `tw-staging-app.tsx` (project copy synced), inserted RIGHT BEFORE the floor hole-fill block (~line 3755). For every `layers.water` column it carves a basin instead of rendering water: rim = lowest NON-water 4-neighbor visible top; `floorY = rim − DIVOT_DEPTH` (default 3, `?divotdepth=N`), **clamped** to ≥1 deep and to keep ≥1 solid cell below the bed (`cb+1`, where cb = column's lowest visible dirt) so it never digs through the whole stack (real scans have validated latent ground below per `canonical_glb_world.py` water-support, so no visible dirt = trust the latent bed). Then it digs visible dirt/grass/dryGrass above `floorY`, lays a watertight dirt bed at `floorY`, and **empties `layers.water`** — so the de-watered footprint reads as floor and the hole-fill (which only counts dirt/grass and would otherwise FLATTEN a de-watered pond up to the rim) leaves the depression intact. Default ON; `?divot=0` restores flat, `?scanwater=1` (raw water) also skips it. Side benefit: emptying `layers.water` before `aoNeighbors` (line ~4368) resolves the stale-AO note from the scan-water-removed entry. RayGI/greedy/compiled-cache all rebuild from the final layers, so they pick up the carve automatically. The runtime **spring is untouched** (it fills an EMPTY water buffer at run time, so this pass never sees spring water). **Verified on the Mac (synthetic world `?terrain=synthetic`, which has a puddle at voxel (38,10)):** (1) `?spring=0` → the puddle footprint is a clean DRY carved divot (stepped brown depression in the grass, no water mesh, `layer==="water"` absent) — pixel-confirmed top-down; (2) spring ON (this world happens to site a spring at voxel [38,12], right at the puddle) → the spring pooled water INTO the carved basin (contained blue pool at world (−0.52,−1.88) = the old puddle), proving it holds water (water can't pool on flat ground). Structural probe: low terrain cluster ~0.2 below median at the puddle. Build passed (5.22s), `get_space_errors` clean. Frames on Telegram. **NOT built — the dependent payoff: rain TEMPORARILY fills divots.** Today divots stay dry unless an existing water source (the spring) reaches them; there is NO rain→pool→evaporate mechanic. That's the next step and needs KJ's design call (fill source: whole-map rain pooling vs. near-source only; + evaporation/drain rate). The spring incidentally filling a nearby divot is permanent-source, not temporary-rain.

- **Voxelizer no longer emits water — carves dry divots at the SOURCE (Jul 25, KJ: "redo the glb voxelizer not to include water"):** KEY FINDING — the GLB voxelizer never classified scan water from the mesh; the worker (`WORKER_CODE` in `src/tw-staging-core.tsx`, a live Space source now mirrored to the project copy) **procedurally FLOODED water into concave floor basins** it seeds itself ("Organic water pooling", ≤`MAX_WATER`=10 pools, `exposed<=2` cols, `basinChance` 0.07/0.12). So "scan water" was a decorative feature, not scan-derived. Change: keep the exact basin SEEDING + flood to find each basin footprint, but instead of pushing water cells, **carve** — lower each basin column's `filledFloor` by `DIVOT_DEPTH` (=2) BEFORE the underside(crust+latent)+surface passes, so the whole column sinks together into a clean watertight dry bowl/channel (2-cell crust = the divot wall). The de-watered basin bed is capped with GRASS like everything else (KJ Jul 25: "should be mostly grass — how it currently works is ok — the waterlogging system should cause lakes to form") — divots are just grassy hollows; the earlier dirt-bed cap was reverted. Lakes FORM at runtime, not in the voxelizer: the spring fills a hollow and the existing waterlogging system recedes the submerged grass, so a lake emerges organically where water collects (mechanism unchanged, per "how it currently works is ok"). `water` array is always empty → no water layer in any fresh scan/upload. Moved the detection ABOVE the underside loop (it previously ran after, so lowering there wouldn't have re-flowed the crust). This SUPERSEDES the runtime carve as the primary path (new scans arrive already-divoted); the runtime `layers.water→divot` pass + `?scanwater`/`?divot` gates stay as legacy handling for saved worlds that already baked water. Build passed (5.15s), `get_space_errors` clean. Verify via the WORKER path (`?debugWorld=1`, which voxelizes `/debug-scan.glb` through `runWorker` — NOT `?terrain=synthetic`, which bypasses the worker): console logs `[voxelizer] divots: N basins carved … water disabled`, and no `layer==="water"` mesh. Look = KJ device check. **Still not built: rain temporarily fills the divots (the payoff) — needs KJ's fill-source + evaporation-rate call.**

- **Spring water capacity 7200 → 50000 (Jul 25, KJ: "the capacity for water needs to be much much larger — let's say 50k"):** `CAPACITY = num("springcap", 50000)` in `water-spring-runtime.ts` (was 7200; project mirror now carries this file too). This is the spring reservoir / waterMesh slot cap — the max standing spring water, so lakes can fill much larger. Safe for load: the pool never prebuilds (`CA_ON` path skips `prime()`), it grows only via the cadence-bounded live tick (`PER_TICK`/`EMIT_MS`), so a big cap can't freeze world load; the InstancedMesh just allocates 50k slots (~3MB matrix, fine on the Mac/native target). `state.capacity` and the mesh both read this const, so it overrides any smaller persisted `meta.spring.capacity`; persisted budget is reused but re-capped to 50k. Override live with `?springcap=N`. Build passed (5.16s), `get_space_errors` clean.

- **Water physics plan — momentum/rivers/rain/erosion (Jul 26, PLAN ONLY, nothing built):** `docs/water-physics-plan.md` is the active plan. Three coexisting layers: occupancy CA (EXISTS, owns mass/lakes) → momentum field (owns rivers/waterfalls/weir overtopping; `momentum=0` ⇒ today's behavior) → erosion (gated, much later, needs explicit KJ go). Key design rulings from the KJ thread: flow rate is NOT depth-driven (calm lakes stay calm) — it's momentum measured from recent cell travel ("last 2–3 frames"); the ledge is a resistance ("minus") that accumulated momentum ("plus") overtops, scaling drain (stream power = discharge × slope); hydraulic jump = friction dumping momentum into deep pools. Phases: 0 slope testbed + `__tw.hydroReport()`, 1 momentum v1 (injection/decay/weir), 2 directional channel bias, 3 flow-aware shader V3 (foam/white water), 4 rain→divot-fill→evaporation (needs KJ calls: fill-source scope, evap rate, rain trigger), 5 erosion (hard-gated, block_events-reversible, never scan architecture). Out of scope permanently: stratification, helical currents, cavitation, meander instability.

Region X-bounds in `meta.hydroRegions`. `__tw.hydroReport()` (in the app, next to `springReport`) wraps the spring report + classifies `waterCells()` into plateau/basin/channel/plain/edge; momentum block reads `springCtrl.momentumReport?.()` else zero-baseline (`{enabled:false,meanSpeed:0,maxSpeed:0,overtopPerSec:0}`) — Phase 1 plugs in with no harness change. Verified on the Mac: spring auto-sites in the basin, water fills→overtops→runs channel→cascades off the edge; hydroReport `basin 1191/channel 400/plain 95/edge 45/plateau 0, culled 10`; frame confirmed (basin pool + edge waterfall) + Telegram.   - **Phase 1 momentum field v1 BUILT + VERIFIED (Jul 26, KJ "Phase one now"):** per-cell `{vx,vz,s}` velocity as an optional `momentum` option on `stepFluid` (`water-spring.ts`) — option absent (`?momentum=0`) ⇒ bit-identical classic CA. Speed is MEASURED front-tracking (KJ ruling): each FALL/SLIDE injects `inject`(1) clamped at `maxSpeed`(6), so consecutive drops compound; rest decays ×0.7/tick, deep pool (≥2 water above = subcritical) ×0.35 = the hydraulic jump (fast flow entering a deep pool dies in ~2 ticks); horizontal moves advect the entry (×0.92, heading blends toward the move). WEIR (KJ's ±): per-mouth drain quota = `DRAIN_CAP + floor(weirK(1)×s)` — stream power, fast water drains more per tick through the same mouth — and the crest-dwell gate: a lip cell with `s ≥ weirRes`(1.2) punches over with NO dwell + counts an overtop; calm water banks and dwells exactly as before (a still lake never bursts, it only genuinely rises over its lip). Runtime (`water-spring-runtime.ts`) owns the field/knobs/stats; report via `momentumReport()` (enabled, trackedCells, meanSpeed over the whole body, maxSpeed, overtopPerSec 10s-window, dropsTotal, overtopsTotal) surfaced in `__tw.hydroReport().momentum`; live knob `__tw.momentum({enabled,inject,frictionRest,frictionDeep,frictionFlow,weirK,weirRes,maxSpeed})` (disable clears the field). Default ON (CA path only). Offline 5/5 in `tools/test_water_momentum.ts` (bun): zero-speed parity BIT-IDENTICAL over 30 mixed ticks, injection compounds+clamps, deep≫rest decay, quota scales with s, crest gate fast-punch vs calm-dwell. Live on the Mac slope bed: 309/3316 cells tracked (only MOVING water carries momentum — calm lake decays out, exactly the design), channel water rides the maxSpeed clamp, overtops ~170/s at the basin lip, water present in basin/channel/plain/edge, live off→0-tracked→re-on verified; frame (night: basin pool + two edge droplet cascades) + Telegram. NOTE: `p1-momentum-slope-*.jpg` in debug-shots/testbed came from same-origin in-page `canvas.toDataURL` POSTed to `/api/testbed-shot` — WORKS and beats the screenshot-file relay (no giant echo; the echo from `agent-browser screenshot` once RESET the browser session mid-run — avoid it, or fully suppress stdout). It persisted because 45 drops/s (=131 particles/s at tier 0.7 vs the ~29k cap) left the whole pool one particle deep for minutes, never reaching the blue depth bands (mid 0.9 / deep 2.8 / abyss 6.8 cells). `DEFAULTS.emitRate` 45→160 in `tinyworld-pwater.ts` (mirror synced) — divot fills into the blue bands in ~1 min; `?pwrate=` / `__tw.pwater({emitRate})` unchanged. Shader untouched (shoreline shallows SHOULD show some bed). Build clean, `emitRate:160` verified in the served app chunk, errors clean. Settled look at full speed = KJ device check. **pwater v1.3 — shallow-checker soften + real-time catch-up + 70%% tier confirmed (Jul 28, KJ: "too checkered — needs to look more like water" / "water movement framerate needs to increase — too slow" / "double check ball size = water test 70%%"):** (1) Shallows block in `fluid-ssfr.ts` softened — caustic web dimmed 0.5→0.16 with soft wide edges (smoothstep 0.60–0.68 → 0.50–0.82) and warped off the sine grid (`cp += vec2(warp*1.9, −warp*1.4)` — kills the perfect diamond lattice), ground read-through washed harder toward the water color (mix 0.62→0.78, brighten 1.15→1.06) and clarity contribution cut 0.7→0.55; depth bands/foam/highlights untouched. (2) Slow-motion water FIXED at the pacing layer: when a device can't sustain 60 solver ticks/s the worker pump previously DROPPED the backlog (`pending %% DT`) so sim time fell behind real time — water visibly ran in slow motion (this, not render fps, was KJ's "framerate"). Now `CATCHUP_BACKLOG = 4·DT`: once the backlog exceeds ~4 frames, ticks run at `DT·2` (stable — the solver's per-substep CFL clamp `vmax = 0.45H/sdt` bounds travel regardless of dt) so sim time tracks real time down to ~30 achieved ticks/s; fast devices never hit the branch (backlog never accumulates) → sandbox-tuned dynamics unchanged. Same catch-up mirrored in the InlineDriver fallback (`sim-driver.ts`). Port parity suite 7/7 after the change (suites call `sim.step` with explicit dt — unaffected). (3) Drop tier CONFIRMED 0.7: `DEFAULTS.scale: 0.7` in `tinyworld-pwater.ts`, threaded via `ctl.scale` to worker reset + renderer particle D; live report on the Mac showed `scale: 0.7` running; only `?pwscale=` overrides. Build clean, minified catch-up ternary verified in the served `sim-worker-*.js`, errors clean. Look + on-device flow speed = KJ device check. **pwater v1.4 — checker KILLED + escalating catch-up + grass greens again (Jul 28, KJ IMG_6246: "still super checkered — get rid of it?" / "make grass green" / "movement framerate still too slow"):** (1) The v1.3 caustic soften was NOT enough — root cause is structural: the divot pool is ~1 particle thick EVERYWHERE, so the whole surface sits at the first depth-band edge (0.9 cells) and the constant-amplitude band warp flips it across that edge every half-wavelength (~4 voxels) → all-over blue/bed checkerboard (the sandbox never showed it: its pools were deep, warp only wobbled shoreline band edges). Fix in `fluid-ssfr.ts` COMPOSITE: caustic web REMOVED entirely (KJ flagged it twice; ripple normals + sparkle glints carry the alive read), warp amplitude scales `smoothstep(0.55, 2.2, thick)` (thin sheets flat, deep pools keep the painterly wobble), and `clarity` reads UNWARPED `thick` (warp is band stylization only — must not modulate bed read-through). Alternatives if shallow texture is wanted later: real projected caustics from the ripple normal field, or a shoreline-ring foam band. (2) Catch-up ESCALATES DT·2 → DT·3 → DT·4 with backlog (1×/1.5×/3× CATCHUP_BACKLOG) in `sim-worker.ts` + `sim-driver.ts` — real-time water holds down to ~15 achieved ticks/s (phone-class) vs ~30 before; the CFL clamp trades top droplet speed, not stability. Port suite 7/7. (3) Grass was tan because pwater STARVED the moisture field: recomputeMoisture read only `springCtrl.waterCells()` (empty — the CA spring never ticks in particle mode) → zero known water → whole field arid. New `waterCells()` on `tinyworld-pwater.ts` (deduped occupied voxel cells from the latest snapshot, ~free at the 8s moisture cadence) feeds recomputeMoisture next to the spring's — irrigation ring greens in ~15s, dryness gradient ramps to ARID_FAR 220, waterlogging sees the pool again. Drop tier re-confirmed 0.7 (`DEFAULTS.scale`, `?pwscale=`). Committed e2b3fb4; build clean, markers verified in the served bundle, errors clean, live report scale:0.7/emit:160. HEADLESS CAVEAT (new): an agent-browser tab is rAF-throttled — the INLINE driver steps ~0.2 ticks/s wall and the worker's 5s no-snapshot fallback MISFIRES (renderFrame never runs to see the snapshot) → headless always reads kind:inline + a never-filling pool; pool-fill/flow-speed/checker verification is DEVICE-ONLY.
  - **GPU compute solver VALIDATED in the sandbox — `?gpu=1` (Jul 29, KJ "can we make these edits?" after the CPU/GPU-split discussion):** `water-sim-sandbox/src/sim/gpu-sim.ts` + `GpuDriver` in `sim-driver.ts` run the PBF substep solve as WebGPU compute — a 1:1 port of the CPU passes (integrate / atomic packed-grid build / 3×(lambda, deltaP, apply+voxel-collide incl. the prev-position tunneling face resolution) / vel-from-positions / XSPH), with the CPU Sim kept as authoritative mirror for emit+back-pressure, evaporation, drain, ledger, report, and foam's sampleFlow via new `host*` hooks in `particles.ts` — semantics and the exact-zero mass ledger unchanged. Solve stage deliberately packed into WebGPU's DEFAULT 8-storage-buffer per-stage limit (flags = nbCount | contact-bit; grid = per-cell [count, 16 slots]; first attempt used 10 buffers and every dispatch silently no-opped — all-zero readback, caught via `window.__gpuLog` uncaptured-error capture) — NO `requiredLimits`, so the same shape ports to iOS later. **Mac Chrome A/B, same scene @ ~3.7k particles: worker 153ms/batch 38tps (1.5k asleep) → GPU 12ms/batch 82tps (0 asleep), identical basin equilibrium 16.2, err 0; frame `water-sim-sandbox/debug-shots/gpu-basin-spill.png` (pool + foam + rim notch) on Telegram.** Sleep system unnecessary on the GPU path (full solve every tick). Ceiling is now the one-round-trip-per-tick readback latency, NOT compute — next levers: batch k ticks per readback (emit is the coupling), then GPU-resident positions for the SSFR renderer. GOTCHAS: WebGPU needs a secure context — `navigator.gpu` is simply absent over plain http (the proxy-to-dev-server URL needed Chrome's `--unsafely-treat-insecure-origin-as-secure` via `agent-browser --args`), and the driver then silently falls back to the worker (check `__ws.snap()`/HUD Engine stat); WebGL canvas screenshots must grab `toDataURL` INSIDE a requestAnimationFrame callback (drawing buffer is cleared after the frame task — a plain eval reads blank white). NOT yet ported into `src/pwater/` (TinyWorld still runs the worker driver); the sandbox validation de-risks that port. The same settings are now ported to TinyWorld `src/pwater/fluid-ssfr.ts` and live staging: depth radius 0.65×, thickness 0.85×1.4, all three smoothing thresholds 1.25×. **Jul 29 water-vanishing fix (KJ "I can see balls but not the fluid — we are over aggressive with hiding"):** thickness+foam splats depth-test at the sphere FRONT POLE (`vViewCenter + vec3(0,0,uRadius)`) instead of the half-buried center, and thin slow-water coverage uses a lower airborne-aware body bar `(0.35s,0.9s)` so one-ball sheets remain visible. The 0.06–0.3s single-droplet eraser floor is unchanged. TinyWorld build passed, live public staging bundle contains `uWorldScale`, `bodyBarLo`, front-pole depth testing, and 1.25× thresholds; project pwater parity/ledger suite remains 7/7. **Not visually verified on the Mac after this port** — the remaining check is an active-device comparison of fluid vs. visible balls at shallow/grazing and waterfall angles.
    - **Falling-water v1.1 — de-cube + densify (Jul 26, KJ: "there are some voxels in the waterfall — and the waterfall is very sparse"):** two fixes in `water-spring-runtime.ts` (project mirror synced). ROOT CAUSE of the cubes: `isFalling` only tested the ONE cell directly below, so a stacked airborne stream rendered every cell but its bottom as a full "pool" cube (the pool mesh). NEW `isFalling` descends through the contiguous water column to its base and returns falling iff the base sits over VOID (no solid support) — so the WHOLE airborne column becomes drop-spray, and resting pool water (bottoms out on solid) still draws as boxes. This also densifies for free (the ex-cube cells now emit spray). Plus: `springsubvox` default 4→6, `springdropcap` 1400→3200 (so the larger falling set isn't starved), droplet vertical spread 0.7→1.05 voxel (consecutive cells' sprays merge into a continuous sheet). **Verified on the Mac slope bed (`?terrain=slope&springcap=50000`):** 1188 live drop instances (was a sparse handful), spanning a continuous vertical band y0.73→−1.28; close crop of the right-edge fall shows all-spray mid-air, zero stacked cubes (only the little lip-pool cubes remain, correct). Frames `debug-shots/testbed/waterfall-{close,densefix}-*.jpg` + Telegram. Build 3.69s, errors clean. Kill/tune via `?springsubvox`/`?springdropcap`. `momentum=0` path unaffected (drop count still scales, cube fix is momentum-independent).
    - **Falling-water v1.2 — 10x spray + blue/white mix + light interaction (Jul 26, KJ: "make spray 10x denser and have mix of blues and whites and interact with the light"):** all in `placeDrop`/`buildDropMesh` constants, `water-spring-runtime.ts` (mirror synced). (1) **10x density:** `springsubvox` 6→60, `springdropcap` 3200→18000, `springdropscale` 0.42→0.28 (finer drops so the 10x count reads as mist, not a wall), horizontal jitter widened via new `DROP_SPREAD` (`?dropspread`, 0.95 voxel). Verified 10,905 live drop instances (was 1188 = 9.2x). (2) **Blue/white MIX — per-drop split, not a speed gradient:** each drop is chosen coherent-blue or aerated-white by hash `hw` vs `whiteChance = BASE_WHITE(0.28) + 0.62·speedFrac` — white drops lerp strongly toward `_dropWhite`(0xdff2ff), blue drops stay near `_dropBlue`(PAL.water) with a faint sheen; faster flow raises the white share but blue always persists, so the curtain never goes monochrome (the earlier speed-only whitening washed the fast cascade all-white). Knobs `?dropbasewhite` (floor white fraction), `?dropwhite` (foam brightness). momentum-off ⇒ speedFrac 0 ⇒ ~28% white / 72% blue (still a mix). (3) **Light interaction:** drops keep the lit `MeshStandardMaterial` but now get a RANDOM per-drop rotation (`dummy.rotation` from hashes hx/hz/hw) so faces catch the sun at varied angles → the curtain shimmers/shades instead of a flat sheet of aligned cubes; material opacity 0.82→0.6 (10x overlap builds density by stacking, so lighter drops), roughness 0.16→0.14 (crisp sun glint). **Verified on the Mac slope bed:** blue+white intermixed per-drop throughout the cascade + visible per-drop brightness variation (light-interactive), frames `debug-shots/testbed/spray-{10x,mix}-*.jpg` + Telegram. Build 4.01s, errors clean. Look/motion = KJ device check.
    - **Falling-water v1.3 — gap fix via downward streak (Jul 26, KJ: "why are there still many gaps? is it because the droplets are created around the original position of voxel?"):** KJ's diagnosis was correct — `placeDrop` clustered all SUBVOX droplets within ±0.5 voxel of the falling cell's grid position (`jy = (i/(SUBVOX-1)−0.5)·1.05·voxel`), and falling cells sit several voxels apart vertically (the CA advances a cell one grid step/tick; the stream is not a solid column), so the space BETWEEN cells was empty = the gaps. Fix in `water-spring-runtime.ts` (mirror synced): each cell's droplets now STREAK DOWNWARD from +0.25 voxel above the cell to `streak` voxels below it — `streak = STREAK_MIN(1.6) + (STREAK_MAX(4.2)−STREAK_MIN)·speedFrac`, a velocity-scaled motion-blur trail so one cell's spray reaches the next falling cell and bridges the gap (fast water streaks longer = physically right). `t=(i+hz)/SUBVOX` marches the lane top→bottom with hz de-correlation (no rigid grid). To keep per-voxel density while spreading over ~4 voxels: `springsubvox` 60→84, `springdropcap` 18000→26000. Knobs `?dropstreakmin`/`?dropstreakmax`. **Verified on the Mac slope bed:** 14,747 live droplets, cascade reads as a CONTINUOUS curtain (no clumps/gaps) top-to-mid, natural dispersion only at the dissipating tail; crop `debug-shots/testbed/spray-streak-*.jpg` + Telegram. Build 4.19s, errors clean. Motion = KJ device check.
    - **Falling-water v1.4 — longer streak + edge/tail mist (Jul 26, KJ: "dropstreakmax slightly better — needs to be even more; drops at the edge need to be smaller/finer like mist"):** `water-spring-runtime.ts` (mirror synced). (1) streak lengthened: `STREAK_MIN` 1.6→2.2, `STREAK_MAX` 4.2→7.5 — the fall reaches much further down as a continuous veil. (2) `placeDrop` size now tapers to mist at the periphery: `tailFine = 1−0.6·t` (finer toward the BOTTOM of each streak) × `edgeFine = 1−0.55·rad` (rad = 2·hypot(hx−0.5,hz−0.5), finer toward the horizontal EDGES), so the coherent core near the lip stays chunky and the tail/edges atomize into fine mist. Knobs `?dropstreakmax`/`?dropstreakmin`, `?dropmist`. **Verified on the Mac slope bed:** long continuous misty veils off both edges (wide + crop `debug-shots/testbed/spray-mist{,-wide}-*.jpg`) + Telegram. Build 4.56s, errors clean.
    - **Falling-water v1.5 — 3x spray amount, length kept (Jul 26, KJ: "spray is too thin now — I like the length but increase amount of spray again by x3"):** the v1.4 streak-lengthening spread the same ~84 droplets over a 7.5-voxel streak → thin. Fix = 3x the droplet count, streak/mist taper unchanged: `springsubvox` 84→252, `springdropcap` 26000→72000. **Verified on the Mac slope bed:** 46,900 live droplets at steady state (was ~14,700 = 3.2×), overtop ~200/s, thick continuous veils at full length. Frames `debug-shots/testbed/spray-3x-*.jpg` + Telegram. Build 4.25s, errors clean. Perf note: ~47k transparent instanced boxes (one draw call) — fine on the Mac target; watch if ported to mobile. Tune `?springsubvox`.
    - **Funnel/spout testbed `?terrain=spout` BUILT + VERIFIED (Jul 26, KJ: "try that funnel and see if it increases spray volume"):** `makeSpoutWorldSource()` in `tw-staging-app.tsx` (mirror synced) + routing wired in the `_mode` chain — a wide catchment bowl (floor y8, outer walls y22) whose side walls converge LINEARLY (corridor half 24→NOTCH_HALF=1 over BASIN_START6→DAM_X50) to a single 3-cell notch (lip y11) in a tall dam (y18), pouring off the dam face onto a low apron (y3) then the +X edge. `hydroRegions` published (basin [6,50], edge [50,64]). **FINDING (answers KJ's question: NO, funnel does NOT increase spray volume — it CONCENTRATES):** on the Mac, water backs up, funnels through the throat as ONE narrow fast coherent jet with momentum PINNED at maxSpeed 6.0 (throat accelerates it to the clamp), z-span only ±0.27 (3-cell wide), y 0.66→−1.62 (tall fall). But total throughput is LOWER than the slope bed's wide lip: overtop ~77/s + 4,345 drops (spout) vs ~200/s + ~14,700 drops (slope wide spill). Reason: a weir passes water ∝ width × per-mouth cap (`DRAIN_CAP + floor(weirK·s)`); the funnel raises the per-mouth cap (faster water) but collapses width to 3 cells — net-negative on throughput — and the HARD ceiling is the source (`PER_TICK`=8 spring emission), so terrain concentrates the feed, can't create volume. Funnel buys concentration+speed (a real coherent waterfall) not volume. Levers for a FATTER jet: raise `?springpertick` and/or widen the notch (`NOTCH_HALF`). Frame `debug-shots/testbed/spout-*.jpg` + Telegram. Build 4.17s, errors clean.
    - **Head-driven outlet — taller fall on pileup BUILT + VERIFIED (Jul 26, KJ: "we need to create a taller waterfall face on pile-up… a pileup should create more VERTICAL surface area"):** `water-spring.ts` (mirror synced) — the per-mouth weir quota gains a head term: `quota = DRAIN_CAP + floor(weirK·s) + floor(headK·excessHeadAboveLip)`, where `headAbove()` measures the water column stacked behind the mouth cell above its lip, clamped by `headMax`(12). So piling water up behind a narrow notch pushes MORE through the same mouth per tick → a taller, faster vertical fall column (vertical surface area), distinct from just widening drain. Defaults `headK:1, headMax:12` ON (KJ ruling: pileups should visibly make taller falls); `momentum=0` path untouched (head term only reads inside the momentum branch). Knobs `__tw.momentum({headK,headMax})`. Offline suite now **7/7** in `tools/test_water_momentum.ts` — zero-speed parity BIT-IDENTICAL preserved (`headK:0` pinned in the parity test) + new test: head above the mouth widens the quota. **Verified on the Mac spout bed:** basin piled to 8,054 cells behind the 3-cell notch, then overtopped as a **tall vertical spray column — 11,916 live drops spanning ~2.2 world-units vertically** (Y −1.62→0.61, ~0.8 wide), overtopS 63, momentum pinned at maxSpeed clamp; blue near the lip fading to white mist down the fall. Frames `debug-shots/testbed/spout-headfall*.jpg` + Telegram. Build passed, `headK` marker in the live bundle, `get_space_errors` clean. Look/feel = KJ device check.
      - **A/B — does the head term increase VOLUME over the lip? MEASURED: NO (Jul 26, KJ: "did volume of water over lip increase? that should be a side effect").** Live-toggle counter-delta on the same piled spout basin (~8005 cells): headK=1 → 82.6 overtop-events/s, **557 drops/s crossing the lip**; headK=0 → 69.5 events/s, **556 drops/s**; basin level identical (8005 vs 8007). So mass flux over the lip is UNCHANGED — the head term raised overtop EVENTS +19% (taller/faster/more energetic fall) but converted the SAME mass into a taller column, it did not add water. **Conservation: at steady state volume-over-lip is capped by the SOURCE (`PER_TICK` spring emission), not the notch — outflow=inflow, basin holds a fixed level regardless of notch discharge capacity.** The head term only increases through-volume in the TRANSIENT (draining a pre-piled reservoir / dam-break surge) or when the notch is a true bottleneck AND the source can supply more. Real levers for fatter sustained flow: raise `?springpertick` or widen `NOTCH_HALF` (matches the earlier funnel finding). headK restored to 1.
      - **CORRECTION — lake LEVEL does draw down with flow force (Jul 26, KJ: "that's not how it should work — if the flow force is high enough it pulls the lake to a lower equilibrium").** The "no level change" above was measured on the SPOUT, which is NOT a bottleneck (it passes inflow right at the lip, so ~0 head banks above the sill → the outlet-strength terms have nothing to act on). On a bed where the outlet genuinely restricts flow, the equilibrium LEVEL responds exactly as KJ said: a stronger/faster outlet passes the same inflow at a LOWER banked head (weir drawdown). Distinction that matters: THROUGHPUT is conserved (= inflow at steady state); the LAKE LEVEL is the free variable and IS set by outlet strength. **Proven offline (test 8/8 in `tools/test_water_momentum.ts`, "stronger flow-force outlet draws the lake to a lower equilibrium level"):** a 3×3 tank, tall containing walls, single 1-col drain hole, identical steady inflow, only the outlet strength varied → equilibrium level **weak (weirK0.2,headK0) y28 / 66 cells → mid (weirK1,headK1 = shipped defaults) y23 / 9 cells → strong (weirK3,headK5) y20 / 9 cells.** So today's shipped CA already draws a bottlenecked lake ~5 cells below a weak outlet — the mechanic is IN, it just needs a restrictive-outlet geometry (or higher inflow) to be visible; the spout was the wrong bed. HARD BOUND: the lake can never draw below the outlet INVERT (the opening's floor) — that's geometric; going below the sill needs the outlet to cut down = erosion (Phase 5, hard-gated). Live-on-Mac drawdown A/B (tighten `NOTCH_HALF` or raise `?springpertick` so head banks, then toggle outlet strength) = not yet run; offline proof stands.
      - **DESIGN PRINCIPLE — the lake LEVEL is emergent from a per-tick mass balance, NEVER a setpoint (Jul 26, KJ: "should not be an arbitrary level — these lakes form out of rules; a level only rises if there's nowhere to go, and even with an outlet a calculation is done: output vs how fast the water is rising").** This is already how the CA works — do NOT add any hardcoded level. The rule: a water cell only leaves if there is somewhere to go (a free cell below, or an outlet mouth still under quota this tick). Inflow that exceeds what the outlet passes has nowhere to go → it stacks → level rises. The outlet's OUTPUT is itself level-dependent — `quota = maxDrainPerCol + floor(weirK·s) + floor(headK·head)` — so as the level (head) rises the outlet passes more, until output catches inflow; that crossover IS the equilibrium (rises while inflow>output, holds when equal, recedes when output>inflow). The knobs `weirK`/`headK` are the OUTLET'S DISCHARGE CHARACTERISTIC (passes-per-unit-head/speed, like a real weir/orifice coefficient), NOT the level. Clean proofs (offline, `water-spring.ts`): (1) RECEDE — fill a pool then cut inflow to 0 → standing water drains to the outlet invert on its own (count→0); nothing pins it. (2) DRAWDOWN — same inflow, only outlet strength varied → weak outlet banks 66 cells high, strong outlet drains to 9. Both are the balance finding its own point. The "faster inflow → higher stable level" corollary is the same rule but noisy to isolate in a single-hole offline tank (all floor water funnels to the one drain — no clean standing pool); the definitive demo is a live bed watching the surface settle at different inflows (not yet run).
      - **Deep-slot canyon outlet `?terrain=canyon` BUILT + PROVEN OFFLINE (Jul 26, KJ: "build a deeper channel outlet in the test world and see if the river flows deeper").** Answers KJ's channel-depth thread: a graded channel with an escape stays a ~1-deep sheet (SPREAD only stacks water ≥2 deep; FALL/SLIDE always win on a slope so it never accumulates) — depth only builds where discharge is forced through less width (conveyance Q = width×depth×velocity). `makeCanyonWorldSource()` in `tw-staging-app.tsx` (mirror synced) + `_mode` routing wired: SAME basin/lip/inflow as the slope bed, but the outlet is one tapered channel cut into a tall `MESA_Y=17` mesa: exactly 4 voxels wide at the basin entrance, narrowing 4→3→2→1, then remaining 1 voxel wide through the low shore with a gentler floor grade (11→7 over 30 vs slope's 11→6 over 22), so the whole discharge is funnelled through a third of the width and backs up deeper. `hydroRegions` published (channel [28,58]). Added a **column-depth metric to `__tw.hydroReport().depth`** (`{cols,maxDepth,meanDepth}` per region) — the direct "does the river run deep" measure, reusable on any hydroRegions bed. **Proven offline in `tools/test_channel_depth.ts` (bun, drives the LIVE `stepFluid` over both heightfields with identical steady inflow=5/tick to a REAL culling steady state — added an open cliff edge so the body doesn't just flood):** slope wide channel mean depth **2.50** over 528 flooded cols; tapered canyon mean depth **3.23** (+29%) confined to 265 cols, and the basin behind the slot backs up higher (11.0 vs 9.0) because the narrow outlet throttles it. So single narrowing outlet ⇒ deeper + more confined river, exactly the physics. Build 6.37s, `WATER CANYON TESTBED` marker in the live bundle, `get_space_errors` clean. **Live look = KJ device check** at `https://kj.zo.space/tinyworld-staging?debugWorld=1&terrain=canyon&springcap=50000` (give the spring ~90s to fill the basin, overtop the lip, and run the slot); `__tw.hydroReport().depth.channel` reads the live depth. NOTE: no Mac capture this session (browser bridge flaky again — profile-lock/sleep-screen); the depth claim is deterministic so offline proof stands, look is KJ's.
      - **HYDROSTATIC lateral rewrite BUILT + OFFLINE-VERIFIED (Jul 27, KJ: "calm lakes should be emergent not by design" → "rewrite from the ground up — depth pressure should also apply, pressure outwards driven by real water physics" → "full hydrostatic").** Rewrote the resting-cell LATERAL rule in `stepFluid` (`water-spring.ts`, mirror + live synced): the old BFS that hunted a distant drop within `spreadRange` (the "by-design" part fighting emergent calm) is replaced by a real **head-gradient** rule, gated `options.hydro` (`?pressure`, default ON; `?pressure=0` = classic BFS, bit-identical). Gravity/drain-quota/weir/momentum/foam UNCHANGED — only the lateral choice changed. THE RULE (below blocked): step toward the lowest-head neighbour, else rest. Lateral allowed iff `canDrop` (neighbour descends at THIS cell's own y → **multi-level discharge** = full hydrostatic: buried water jets out a side opening at depth, not only over the rim) OR neighbour surface **≥2 lower** (genuine leveling; 1-lower is skipped = would only swap the bump → churn). No lower-head neighbour ⇒ no move ⇒ **CALM emerges** (level/±1 surface is the fixed point; no `carryMin`/`biasMin`/speed threshold — the `carryMin` forward-carry stopgap was written then REVERTED same turn). Depth-pressure is emergent, NOT a knob: a per-column shed-quota was tried + removed (grid gravity refills a column vertically the instant it sheds → quota inert, and it's the tuned param KJ rejected); depth pushes harder purely because a deeper body has more submerged levels each discharging. **Offline 5/5 `tools/test_water_hydro.ts` (bun):** level lake bit-still (calm, no threshold), deep tank drains through a mid-depth SIDE hole to the hole level (multi-level), mass conserved (closed box), lopsided pile spreads→STILL equilibrium, `hydro:false`==classic. Momentum suite still 8/8 (parity). Live: `?pressure`, `__tw.pressure(true/false)`, `__tw.hydroReport().momentum.hydro`. Build 5.36s, shipped to shared staging+prod, `get_space_errors` clean, `"pressure"` marker in the live bundle. **KNOWN NUANCE (confirm on device/canyon):** per-tick throughput through a 1-wide same-level notch is 1 cell/level (physical for a fixed opening) — a channel runs deep because a deep lake submerges the opening across MANY levels, not because one notch passes many/tick. If the canyon channel still reads thin, next lever = **simultaneous/frozen-state update** (decide all moves vs the tick-start snapshot so an upper cell sheds forward at its level instead of falling into the just-vacated hole = true "both at once"; bigger change, breaks naive free-fall, own design pass). **Look/feel + two-deep-channel = KJ device check** at `https://kj.zo.space/tinyworld-staging?debugWorld=1&terrain=canyon&springcap=50000` (`?pressure=0` = instant A/B revert to old CA).

- **Worlds reset + debug world re-voxelized (Jul 25, KJ: "delete all worlds — I'll reupload myself — only keep the debug world and revoxelize that one"):** the live DB (`data/tinyworld.db`, the file the Space `/api/tinyworld-worlds` route reads — `DB_PATH = ${DATA_DIR}/tinyworld.db`) had 4 worlds. KEY FINDING that drove the reset: a saved world's payload is NOT just voxelizer output — it carries server-planted trees (`plant_world_trees.py`) + accumulated player state (spring/ledger/knowledge/playerState/void/clock), so a naive from-GLB re-voxelize would strip trees + progress. KJ's cleaner call: delete the 3 real worlds (Room + 2× leslielab) — he re-uploads them through the normal flow, which auto-applies the new divots/no-water voxelizer + tree planting — and re-voxelize only `debugscan` (`w_mruu7kpe_9vwzy5`). Done: full DB backup at `data/backups/pre-delete-revoxel-<ts>/`; deleted the 3 worlds across all 7 `world_id` tables (`worlds,world_blocks,glb_uploads,block_events,workers,world_zones,pending_captures,world_geo_cal`) + orphan bridges; live API confirmed 1 world left.
