Re-voxelize path (reusable): `TW_APP=/__substrate/space/src/tw-staging-core.tsx bun tools/scan-siting/extract-voxelizer.ts` regenerates `voxelizer.mjs` from the CURRENT worker (with divots), then `bun tools/scan-siting/revoxel-one.mjs <world_id>` voxelizes the stored GLB (`glb_uploads.path`), serializes layers to base64 Int32 (excludes the `ground` layer — the app rebuilds it), writes `{layers,meta}` to `world_blocks` preserving old anchor/name meta but CLEARING tree/finalize/player-state flags, then `python3 tools/plant_world_trees.py <world_id>` plants trees. **Verified on debugscan:** water=0 (10 basins carved), 75 trees planted (trunks 6246/leaves 17026), blockCount 222,972, spring/playerState cleared. Loading the saved world visually needs the geo-gate (node override at its anchor 40.7297,-73.9954) — structural proof stands; device look = KJ. NOTE: the ephemeral `?debugWorld=1` path already always voxelizes fresh with the new worker, so it needed no migration.

## Building reference grammar (Jul 13)

- Nine unique voxel building references are normalized under `assets/building-references/source/`; the two supplied Small Building 03 archives were identical duplicates.
- `docs/building-reference-portfolio.md` is the source of truth for extracting their visual language into functional worker plans: invariants, style grammar, optional moves, five developmental generations, and the plan-before-mesh pipeline.
- Reference buildings may be solid or non-enterable. Never copy them literally: generated structures require supported foundations, hollow rooms, real entrances, navigable doors, supported floors, weatherproof roofs, and no floating decoration.

## Verification default (updated Jul 25)

- **Primary live-verification surface: real Chrome on KJ's connected MacBook (`Kevins-MacBook-Air.local` via `use_device`).** It has a real GPU and reliably renders this heavy WebGL app. Zo Browser and local `agent-browser`/headless Chromium repeatedly wedge on world loading or render blank WebGL — use them only for quick secondary diagnostics (console logs, Space health), never as the deciding visual check. This supersedes the earlier "Zo Browser first" default.
- **iOS Safari is NOT the shipping target and NOT a design budget (KJ, Jul 25).** The product ships as a native iPhone app (cloud-hosted/rendered, phone as thin client). Safari's GPU/jetsam ceiling is a dev-surface constraint only: keep favoring fidelity (RayGI, GTAO, bounce rig) and do not optimize against Safari memory limits. KJ's iPhone checks judge look and feel, not Safari survival.
- **Drone shares the Sentinel chase camera (Jul 25, KJ: "same distance so camera is maintained"):** the old hull-ahead rig (hull 9.5v in front of a stationary camera) is gone for flight. Now `_chaseDrone` runs the drone through the SAME chase block as the Sentinel: hull flies AT the body anchor, lens trails `SENTINEL_TARGET_H×3.2×camdist` behind on a TRUE sphere (lens exactly followDist from the hull at orbit yaw/pitch — this makes lookAt pitch == orbit pitch, so the desktop pitch recapture `targetLookRef.x = camera.rotation.x` is drift-free; the Sentinel keeps its height-biased rig and yaw-only recapture). Same occlusion march/anti-clip; drone extras: `droneFwdPull` extends the LENS distance (speed = camera pulls back), rising-edge pitch seed −0.24 (level pitch at hover height buried the lens in grass), and drone ground clearance `voxel×3.2` (grass blades are instanced, invisible to the voxel march; 0.6v filmed pure grass — both failure modes hit on the testbed before the fixes). Two dead ends tried first and reverted: hull-ahead at 40v buries in walls; + occlusion clamp slams hull to a giant dark close-up at min distance. Parked drone now parks AT its stored pose (no forward offset). Effective pitch orbit range is the chase clamp [−1.25, +1.05] (slightly tighter than the old free-look PI/2.2). Verified on testbed: droneNdc (0,0), full silhouette from above-behind at Sentinel distance.
- **Verification testbed (Jul 25):** `https://kj.zo.space/tinyworld-testbed` = one stable URL that loads the debug world and auto-embodies the Sentinel (`?embody=drone` for the Drone); all other params (raygi/camdist/tod/...) pass through to staging. Backed by `?embody=` handling at the ready transition in `tw-staging-app.tsx` (goes through the `setPilotMode` world-ready gate, never bypasses it). **Screenshot retrieval bridge:** `agent-browser --session tb ... screenshot /tmp/x.jpg` on the Mac, then a second session (`--session relay --profile /tmp/tw-relay-0725`) opens `file:///tmp/x.jpg`, canvas-encodes it, and POSTs base64 to `/api/testbed-shot?token=tw-shot-7f2b91` (`mode:'no-cors'`, content-type text/plain — a custom header would trigger a CORS preflight and fail from the `null` origin). Files land in `debug-shots/testbed/`. Mac gotchas: device only permits `agent-browser *` commands; each new session needs its own `--profile` or it hits the singleton profile lock; evals time out while the world build saturates the main thread (wait ~70s and retry).

Consolidated from design threads (Gemini brainstorm "Project Quantum" Mar 2026 + Zo build sessions Jun 2026). This file is the source of truth for project state. Update it as the project evolves.

## What this is

Persistent cloud-hosted voxel world sim for iPhone. **Architecture (corrected):** the iPhone LiDAR-scans a physical space **on device**; the captured world is uploaded, hosted, and simulated/rendered **in the cloud**; gameplay is delivered back to the phone (rendered in-cloud, phone as thin client). **There is NO live AR camera-overlay mode** — the phone is a scanner + a play client, not an AR viewport. The scan becomes a Minecraft-like voxel "tiny world" (12×12 blocks/sq ft) populated by semi-autonomous AI "Tiny People," under threat from "The Void." Worlds are geo-anchored; connecting two worlds requires physically scanning the path between them ("bridging"). Formerly called **Project Quantum**.

## Key design decisions (settled)

1. **Worlds are data, not processes.** No per-world VMs. Worlds = rows in SQLite (`data/tinyworld.db`), simulated lazily on access (catch-up sim: elapsed time × void rate × weather). Ports to Postgres/Supabase later. See `docs/persistence-and-access.md`.
2. **Geography is the auth layer.** Access via geo gate (GPS within 150 m of anchor) OR a live bridge (integrity > 0). Bridges decay ~8 pts/day unless re-scanned; anchor structures (cairn → lidar_tower) slow decay. No accounts in prototype. **REAFFIRMED Jun 27: the owner is NEVER exempt — no owner-bypass, no `?owner=` shortcut, nothing that hides the gate behind account ownership.** A wrong owner-bypass was added to `/api/tinyworld-worlds` and the staging frontend and immediately reverted; do not reintroduce it. The only testing affordance is the node override `?nodeLat=&nodeLon=` (forces a GPS-tier node at a REAL anchor coord — the gate still evaluates normally). Gotcha: a coarse/foreign egress IP (observed resolving to London on Jun 27) hides all NYC-anchored worlds even for the owner — that is the gate working, not a bug. Fix is GPS-in-range or the node override, never an owner exemption.
3. **Voxel pipeline core = OpenMVS/GLB finalizer only.** The canonical saved-world path is phone video capture -> COLMAP/OpenMVS mesh -> gravity-aligned GLB -> TinyWorld GLB voxelizer. Use `tools/old_path_from_capture.py` as the project-owned entrypoint and `tools/glb_to_world_payload.py` as the GLB-to-world payload converter. The old Depth-Anything/RGB-D/TSDF path has been removed from the active project and must not be used as fallback/preview/debug. Keep `dda-voxelize` concepts only as legacy/reference naming if useful. The user-facing path remains camera capture inside `/tinyworld`, but its final world must be the OpenMVS/GLB payload. Phased/progressive reveal may exist as UX later, but not via Depth/TSDF and not as a saved-world builder.
4. **Semantics from GPS, geometry from LiDAR.** LiDAR gives shape only; GPS unlocks the semantic layer (OSM landuse + ESA WorldCover + Köppen climate + Open-Meteo weather + date/season) → block palette. v1 accepts raw geometry + biome palette (no surface classifier).
5. **Void follows real time.** Night (8pm–6am local) = aggressive; winter = +25%. Biome sets hostility + creature archetypes. NYC: Soot Crawlers (roads), Girder Shades (walls), Steam Wraiths (waterfront), Concrete Nulls (rare). See `docs/biome-nyc-temperate.md`.
6. **Turn-based action/pause cycle** to absorb cloud latency. User builds = blueprint; Tiny People execute it (delayed). Combat turn-based.
7. **Tiny People (Ghibli Aesthetic)**: commandable, not controllable. Moving away from voxel geometry toward **pixelated Studio Ghibli-style sprites** (billboarded in the 3D world). MBTI-style archetypes; happiness/longevity/independence metrics; voice commands; they talk back as the UI. Pocket dimension: carry them on-device, deploy in new scans; abandoned ones navigate home. See `docs/tiny-people.md`.
8. **The Supplier (The Sentinel)**: A massive 10-15 block tall entity. It "wakes up" when the user is controlling the world and sits back down to become a static part of the landscape when the user leaves. Acts as the world's protector and source of raw materials. See `docs/sentinel-supplier.md`.
9. **Scan composition via tectonics (Jun 13).** Scans compose into a single geo-anchored world, not isolated islands. Geo-tag is the canonical anchor; one scan per region (3 exceptional re-scan cases); seams between adjacent scans are where tectonic events happen and where builds are at risk; interior is permanent. Manual coarse + ICP fine alignment on submission. Displaced mass flows to the Void pool, ledger conserved. See `docs/scan-composition-and-tectonics.md`. **Conflicts with #2's "each scan = own world" model — bridges remain as access control but the underlying geometry unifies; schema migration deferred until implementation starts.**
10. **World simulates lightly while the player is away (Jun 23).** When the Sentinel sleeps / the player leaves, the world remains alive: villagers continue to act, small builds can progress, and the Void creeps slowly. Returning should feel like time passed and the survivors had inner life, but the offline sim must be bounded and legible: no huge irreversible surprises without recap, warning, or player agency.
11. **Stockpile must be visible, not an invisible inventory (Jun 23).** "What blocks you see is what you get." The ledger pool can still be named STOCKPILE internally, but it must represent physical piles, carried blocks, crates, carts, or villager-held materials in the world. There is no gifted/free building, including onboarding. Every player or worker build must consume visible existing blocks from the world, a carried block, or a visible local stockpile. If the player cannot see or locate the material, it cannot be spent.
12. **Photorealistic 3D Tiles are phantom context until scanned (Jul 6).** Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles may be downloaded/cached once per area and reused as a non-reactive dead-globe substrate: buildings/terrain can appear as ghosted, walkable-enough reference mass, but they are not scanned world matter. They cannot be harvested, counted in the conservation ledger, used for build cost, treated as proof of ownership/access, or made fully interactive until the player scans/reclaims that space. This preserves the single-planet GIS/void feel without letting external map data bypass the scan economy.
13. **Mechs are a shared collectible settlement fleet (Jul 13).** TinyWorld supports many buildable and recoverable mech frames, not one permanent mech per mote and not only three fixed models. Frames belong physically to the settlement; motes temporarily crew whichever available machine fits the task, their tier, skills, personality, condition, and relationships. Individual motes can develop preferences and mastery with particular frames without owning them. Houses may add tiny driveways, charging pads, sheds, or workshops, while larger settlements build communal parking lots and a hangar that visibly records progression. Mechs specialize across excavation, hauling, construction, farming, salvage, scouting, patrol/combat, and ship repair; parts can be mixed, repaired, upgraded, traded, recovered, or lost. Destroying a mech hurts its crew and may leave a wreck, but does not erase a surviving mote. See `docs/worker-growth-mechs-and-food.md` §5.2–5.4.

## Current state (Jun 2026)

- **Compiled-world browser cache v1 (Jul 24):** `src/compiled-world-cache.ts` stores exact greedy wall/ground chunk position, normal, color, and index buffers in IndexedDB. `wall-greedy.ts` and `ground-greedy.ts` can hydrate cached chunks while retaining their existing runtime neighbor-aware dirty-chunk rebuild path; `tw-staging-app.tsx` fingerprints the final post-gravity voxel layers plus compiler/palette/AO settings, loads matching buffers, and stores a miss in idle time after all build-time edits flush. Any voxel/config/version change invalidates safely. This removes greedy wall/ground recompilation on unchanged repeat loads without changing fidelity or authoritative world data. Current boundary: a persisted geometry change creates a new whole-world fingerprint, so the first load after that change recompiles the greedy cache; cross-revision per-chunk reuse is the next cache version. Project-owned copies of the three live Space sources are in `src/`; Space build passed and `/tinyworld` + `/tinyworld-staging` use the shared app. Zo Browser verification timed out in world loading, so miss→hit timing remains a bounded manual check; Space errors are empty.

- **World-ready embodiment lock (Jul 23):** TinyWorld now marks the transition as `loading` before saved-world fetches, debug-scan fetch/voxelization, GPS auto-loads, and golden-playtest preparation begin. Sentinel/Drone embodiment is blocked both in the UI and inside `enterWalkRef`/`__tw.setPilotMode` until `phaseRef === "ready"`; automated playtests explicitly wait for readiness and `__tw.worldReady()` exposes the same gate. This closes the stale-scene handoff window where an old ready scene could accept embodiment while its replacement was still being prepared.

- **Mobile machine HUD + camera centering (Jul 21):** The iPhone LASER control no longer touches scene-local `beamHeld`/`cancelCharge` variables from React (the live browser logged `Can't find variable: beamHeld`, which was the actual reason mobile firing failed). `machineBeamInputRef` is now the stable React→current-scene bridge; the button uses captured Pointer Events and hold/release drives continuous charge/carve safely across world rebuilds. Hopper UI is a single bottom text line with inline material selectors—no panel. Sentinel chase default `camlat` is 0, and the Drone is placed directly on `camera.getWorldDirection()` every frame (position lag removed; pitch/roll spring retained), eliminating rightward camera-turn drift. A later real light-debug embodiment exposed a separate vertical offset that the old X-only check missed: Sentinel measured `NDC y=-0.674` because the chase aimed at `0.86×` visible height plus a default `+0.55H` bias, and Drone measured `NDC y=-0.195` because its center-ray anchor was lowered `0.8v`. The chase now aims at the Sentinel's measured visible midpoint with default `camup=0`, Drone `droneDrop=0`, and `framingReport()` uses the same measured Sentinel midpoint. Production `/tinyworld` and staging share this app.

- **Drone proportional scale (Jul 22):** Doubled the authored Drone target length from `2.4v` to `4.8v` so it matches the enlarged machine proportions without changing its center-ray anchor. MacBook pixel checks at 1440×900 and 390×844 show the hull centered; portrait framing is intentionally tight with the outer wing tips touching the side edges. Build passed and Space errors were empty.

- **Latent cells are first-class dig targets (Jul 21):** The shared staging app routes player ray hits, mobile fallback, build-mode picks/harvests, Tiny Person mining/pickup/pry, and blast removal through `resolveBlockAt(x,y,z)`. It resolves visible slotMap blocks, hiddenMap blocks, and latent dirt/interior cells. A latent hit materializes through an overflow dirt/wall slot, marks the exact coordinate `spent`, removes via the ordinary `removeBlockFrom` path, refreshes the latent prism immediately, and asserts that no unspent or visible materialized latent cell remains at that coordinate. Production `/tinyworld` uses this shared app. The Space build passes and `get_space_errors` is clean; use Zo Browser for device-level tunnel/dig verification.


- **Staging PROMOTED to production `/tinyworld` (Jul 20):** KJ: "promote staging into production." The two apps had fully forked — `/tinyworld` (production) ran `tinyworld-app.tsx` (~399 KB, from BEFORE this whole line of work) and had NONE of the subsystems the recent fixes are built on (no water spring — water was static baked blobs; no latent interiors/prisms; no floater survey/gravity settle; simpler worker). `/tinyworld-staging` ran `tw-staging-app.tsx` (~1.07 MB) with all of it. So there was no per-pass "port" — the honest, reversible promotion was to repoint the route. **Change:** the `/tinyworld` space route now mirrors the staging route — `import App from "@/tw-staging-app"` wrapped in the same `useEffect` that sets `__tw.sentStop.settleDur = 1` (was `import App from "@/tinyworld-app"; export default App;`). One line, snapshot saved at `/home/.z/workspaces/con_OfeHIgHnAMqjtVRZ/rollback/tinyworld-route-PRE-PROMOTION.tsx`; revert = write that back. Both routes already share the `/api/tinyworld-worlds` store (+ staging also uses `-stt`/`-upload-glb`, both live), so saved worlds/state/workers carry over — no data migration. Verified: (1) build — `/tinyworld` route chunk `tinyworld-DolPSk7n.js` imports `tw-staging-app-CQLr96ma.js`; (2) runtime — console under `/tinyworld?debugWorld=1` (a staging-only path prod never had) ran the full pipeline: `spring active`, `gravity settle (pre-mesh) voided 1136 landed 824 swept 795`, `latent gravity settle pruned 43`, `floater sweep deleted 0 (water-only)`. `get_space_errors` clean. Headless agent-browser wedged (blank shot / detached eval context — known failure mode for this heavy WebGL app), so the VISUAL on `/tinyworld` is a KJ device check; the console is the authoritative functional proof. NOT touched: `/tinyworld-staging` (unchanged, still a valid staging surface) and `/tinyworld/capture` (still imports the old capture UI — if the promoted app links into capture it hits the stale route; flagged, secondary flow). Promoting also makes ALL staging-only systems live at once (void v10/v11, day-night, worker spirits, latent interiors, merge/anchor, multiworld) — several were "pending KJ device check," now live on production.

- **Pre-mesh gravity settle — floaters cleared BEFORE anything renders (Jul 20, `src/tw-staging-app.tsx`):** KJ: "why do these blocks exist even on first load? not cache." ROOT CAUSE (traced, not guessed): the runtime **vegetation hole-fill pass** (`~line 3336`, right before the `addLayer` calls) plants grass/dirt onto scanned surfaces AFTER the worker's own cleaning — exactly the "vegetation happens after voxel cleaning" KJ flagged — so no worker-side sweep ever sees it, and the load-time `settleFloaters` (connectivity delete) only removed them at the END of a ~60–90 s build, so they were VISIBLE the whole load. Diagnosis also corrected a wrong model: most of these grass/dirt "floaters" are NOT physically floating — they rest on **occluded (hidden/latent) dirt** that is never drawn, so they only LOOK floating; gravity can't drop a supported block, so those must be swept, while genuinely airborne ones fall/void. FIX: new `gravitySettleLayers(layers)` runs on the FINAL layer buffers (veg + regenerated trees included) **right before the first `addLayer`** — so a floater is fixed before it ever meshes. Floater detection = **VISIBLE-only** 6-connected flood (matches the eye); largest component = ground; each smaller pure-terrain component obeys gravity — rigid drop bottom-up, landing on the **full physical solid** (visible + hidden + protected). Lands & reconnects → kept as debris; drops past the lowest solid with nothing to catch it → **removed from the buffer before meshing** (never enters the conservation baseline, so disappearance *is* the ledger move — no `ledgerMove` needed); a **visible-connectivity finisher** sweeps anything that landed still-isolated (this is what removes the grass-on-hidden-dirt, which falls 0). Trees (`trunks/leaves/fruit/seed/sapling`) + water + `hidden_dirt` are anchors: never moved/deleted, and they ground whatever rests on them; components >`CAP`=1024 kept (real detached architecture). Disable: `?gravsettle=0`. Live-verified on `?debugWorld=1` (the ceiling scan): pass logs `voided 1136, landed 824, finisher swept 795; main 196870`, and the downstream `settleFloaters` then finds only **2 comps / 5 water blocks (deleted 0)** — i.e. it's now a near no-op because nothing terrestrial floats. `floaterReport()` = water-only; wide shot = one cohesive island, no scattered cubes in the void (main mass unchanged ~197k, so nothing real over-deleted). The `tw-staging-core.tsx` `WORKER_CODE` also carries a redundant worker-side gravity+connectivity pass (validated offline true-solid = 0) as early cleanup of worker-origin floaters; the runtime pre-mesh pass is the authoritative one. Offline diagnostics: `tools/scan-siting/floater-origin.mjs` (wall-clustering census — 91% of floaters hug walls, from the up-facing classifier misfiring on tilted scan geometry) and `gravity-settle.mjs` (fall/void split sim). **Debris LOOK on device is a KJ check** (824 landed blocks rest as terrain; headless can't judge whether that reads clean).

- **Latent prisms follow the physics model too (Jul 20, `src/tw-staging-app.tsx` `gravitySettleLatent`):** KJ pointed at a "clump of five dirt blocks" floating in open sky (`IMG_6082`). Diagnosis (verified, not guessed): the VISIBLE voxel layers were census-clean (only 2 water floaters, main 197,624) — `gravitySettleLayers` had already cleaned them. The floater was a **latent-underside prism**, NOT a voxel clump. The latent fill (`renderLatentFillPrisms`, `latentRef.current.cols` = per-column `[y0,y1]` ranges decoded from the worker's `meta.latentCols`) renders as a **separate InstancedMesh group** (`layer:"latentFill"`, colored by `meta.latentLayer`) with NO slotMap and NO voxel identity, so gravity settle + the floater sweep never touched it AT ANY SIZE. A stray elevated floor sample (same up-facing scan noise) spawns a floating latent column; when gravity settle sweeps its surface voxel, the prism underneath is orphaned and hangs in the air. PROOF it's a prism: `?latentfill=0` (disables only prism render) removes the floating solids while the voxel world stays byte-identical (same 197,624 + 2 water). FIX: new `gravitySettleLatent(layers, latent)` runs after `gravitySettleLayers`, after the latent decode, **before the prisms mesh**. Applies the SAME connectivity model to the latent layer: integer-keyed 6-connected flood over the COMBINED solid (visible settleable voxels from the post-settle layer buffers + live latent cells, minus `spent`); largest component = ground; any latent cell not connected to it (and its component ≤`CAP`=1024) is a floater. Whole disconnected column → dropped from `latent.cols`; partially disconnected → stray cells marked `spent` (the latent "removed" state — prism render, physics/tunnel, and save all skip `spent`). Disable: `?gravsettle=0` (shares the voxel-pass flag). Live-verified on `?debugWorld=1` (ceiling scan): logs `pruned 43 floating latent columns (93 cells); main 1070548`, prisms drop 32,514 → 32,471, underside mass intact (no over-prune). **Verification caveat:** the rendered-scene census CANNOT cross-check this — walls are **greedy-meshed** (102,780 voxels → merged triangles, no per-voxel instances), so a rendered-voxel flood fragments wherever a wall bridged and massively over-reports disconnection. The pass floods the true **layer buffers** (all wall voxels present pre-greedy), so its own log is authoritative. **KJ device check:** the specific floater at his close angle — headless wide shot shows a cohesive island with no sky floaters, but a prism that's technically 6-adjacent to a wall (attached-but-protruding) would be KEPT by pure connectivity; if such a stub remains it needs a surface-protrusion rule, not connectivity.

- **Edit-time floater physics — strand → fall into void → delete (Jul 19, chunk `tinyworld-staging-H3tUrVLL.js`):** The `<3` tiny-clump cleanup now fires on EDIT, not just on load. Root context: `settleIslandAt` (the drop-as-a-unit beat, already wired to every dig/harvest/mine via `removeBlockFrom → queueSupportCheck → processSupportQueue`) previously only RELOCATED a stranded small island onto the terrain below; a `<3` clump over the void or above a gap either sheeted down or lingered, and the DELETE of tiny clumps only ran in the load-time `settleFloaters` sweep. Added a branch in `settleIslandAt`: an ungrounded island of size ≤ `editFloat.max` (default 2 → components of 1–2) is DETACHED synchronously (colMap removed, slotMap deleted, `ledgerMove("world","void")`, protection/grass updated) and the instance VISIBLY FALLS into the void (`editFloat.voidDrop`=34 cells over `voidMs`=900 ms, reusing the `fallingBlocks` beat with a new `void:true` flag) before its slot is freed in `animateFallingBlocks`; larger 3..cap chunks keep the old relocate-onto-terrain behavior. Veg exempt (`trunks/leaves/fruit/seed/sapling` — a real canopy legitimately floats over an island edge); water/immovable already exempt. **Place path needs no hook — `placeAt` rejects floating placement (requires an occupied neighbour or ground), so a player can never mint a mid-air block.** KJ's trigger call (he described "drop unsupported into the void and then gets deleted"): tiny-clump scope + visible beat are the DEFAULTS. Knobs: `?editfloat=0` off (pure relocate, old behavior), `?editfloatmode=any` (drop ANY stranded island into the void, not just <3), `?editfloatmax=N` (tiny ceiling), `?editfloatinstant=1` (skip the fall beat, delete immediately); live `__tw.editFloater({on,mode,max,instant})`, `__tw.editFloaterReport()`, and a synchronous self-test `__tw.editFloaterTest()` (borrows one real slot, mints + strands a floater, restores the borrow — net ledger-neutral). Verified on the debug world: `editFloaterReport()` = `{on:true,mode:"tiny",max:2,instant:false}`, `editFloaterTest()` = `{ok:true,spawned:true,detached:true,deletedDelta:1}` in BOTH tiny and any modes, 0 console errors. **The visible fall-into-void animation is a KJ device check** — transparent falling voxels don't render legibly in headless Chromium; the DETACH + ledger + count are proven synchronously, the beat's LOOK is on device. Conservation-safe (world→void). Applies to production + staging (shared file).

- **Spring-siting regression harness (Jul 19):** `tools/scan-siting/` voxelizes a real scan through the app's exact `WORKER_CODE` (extracted at runtime, no drift) and runs the mirrored siting/flow logic offline — test spring/voxelizer changes without a device check. `bun tools/scan-siting/test.ts` = PASS/FAIL (fixture `fixtures/debug-scan-small.glb`, asserts natural-basin + over-lip, latent-underside containment, and flow-throttle bounds; dry & raining); `bun tools/scan-siting/inspect.mjs [glb]` = height map + BASIN_BAND sweep + siting; `bun tools/scan-siting/latent-contain.mjs` = raw-shell vs latent-solid below-floor comparison. **`extract-voxelizer.ts` now pulls `WORKER_CODE` from `tw-staging-core.tsx`** (the worker the live `?debugWorld` scan actually runs), NOT `tinyworld-app.tsx` — those are two DIFFERENT workers (staging is ~2× bigger with the wall/floor/ceiling rectification passes); the old default silently tested against the wrong voxelizer. Override with `TW_APP=…`. Self-contained (own `three`; `node_modules`/`voxelizer.mjs` gitignored). This is the canonical small-scan test asset.
- **Real scan = the PRIMARY live debug world (Jul 19):** `?debugWorld=1` on the staging app (`https://kj.zo.space/tinyworld-staging?debugWorld=1`) now loads the real small scan `/debug-scan.glb` (a space asset, same GLB as the `scan-siting` fixture) instead of a synthetic world — it reproduces real-scan bug classes (crater holes, outlier spikes, thin shells) the code-generated beds hid. The load effect in `tw-staging-app.tsx` fetches the asset, voxelizes it through the EXACT upload `runWorker`, then `buildWorld({layers,meta,...})` ephemerally (no server save / ICP merge); `meta.debugWorld:true` so it's never persisted and no super-tree is grafted. **The debug scan now carries the LATENT UNDERSIDE like a real upload (Jul 19):** the branch passes `result.latentCols`/`latentTotal`/`latentLayer` into meta (base64 quads), so the floor is watertight (solid from the walkable floor down) and the shell renders as solid fill prisms — walls dam water and water can't drip through the floor crust, fixing the "thin-shell leak" where scan floors are a 1-voxel crust with air beneath. `?rawshell=1` drops it to reproduce the old hollow-shell leak. The current asset is a real interior ROOM scan WITH A CEILING (`debugscan-dcd6e8c3a49d.glb`, span 4.39m, ~35.6k cols, ceiling y≈178, floor y≈−8), a better regression target than the old outdoor crater. Live: latent interiors = 32,514 columns / 875,342 cells; spring sites natural-basin `origin [-9,0,-70]`, pool bounded at `floorY −26`, bbox 23×19×16. Offline containment (`latent-contain.mjs`): below-floor drainage 127→24 columns with latent on. NB: `buildWorld(File)`'s `source instanceof File` branch is effectively dead — it reads `source.meta` (line ~3312) which a raw File lacks (`treesUpgraded` crash), so always hand `buildWorld` a built OBJECT, never a File. Alternatives: `?terrain=synthetic` (old torture world — floaters/overhang), `?terrain=hydro` (synthetic hydrology bed — basins/cliffs/waterfall), `?terrain=scan&scan=/other.glb` (any other uploaded scan asset). To swap the debug scan, replace BOTH the `/debug-scan.glb` space asset and `tools/scan-siting/fixtures/debug-scan-small.glb` — live and offline share the same GLB by design.
- **Sub-voxel feathered water surface (Jul 19):** Water is simulated as whole voxels (conservation + `stepFluid`/`emitAndSettle` depend on the integer grid) but the VISIBLE surface height is decoupled from the voxel. The exposed top cell of any pool (no water directly above) renders as a slab whose height is a fraction of a voxel, floored to the voxel bottom; the fraction feathers CONTINUOUSLY from `WATER_THIN` (default 0.4) in the pool interior down to `WATER_THIN*WATER_EDGE` (default 0.3 → 0.12) at the rim, keyed to how many of the 4 horizontal neighbours are water (smoothstep) — the neighbour-averaged surface Minecraft uses so a fluid tapers at its edge instead of a hard cube. Submerged cells stay full so deep pools fill solid. Applied in three render paths via a shared `waterHFrac()`: `setCell(hFrac)` + `reconcilePool` (spring/live CA; tracks a numeric `waterThin` map quantised to 0.02 and re-renders a cell when the pool rises/drains/ripples over it) in `water-spring-runtime.ts`; the `addLayer` water loop (static/scan-baked) and `settleWaterNear` (edit-time re-pour, re-renders the region's tops) in `tinyworld-app.tsx`. Render-only — colMap, ledger, budget, conservation untouched. Tune live: `?waterthin=N` (interior film, 1 = old full-cube) and `?wateredge=N` (rim taper). Limit (flagged, not built): truly continuous *depth* (a 1-deep puddle deepening smoothly toward a 2nd layer) needs sub-cell mass in the sim (W-Shadow/Game-of-Flow model), which would break integer conservation; the feather is the safe render-side analog. Film look needs a device check — headless renders translucent water too faintly.
- **Per-mouth drain cap + source-relative cull floor (Jul 19):** KJ's "each hole/edge has a max drain speed" fix for water drilling through thin scan shells. `stepFluid` (`water-spring.ts`) gained `maxDrainPerCol` (default ∞ = old behaviour): a per-tick ledger caps how many cells may descend through one `(x,z)` mouth; rules FALL(1)/SLIDE(2) consume a mouth quota and a saturated straight-down mouth pushes the cell into SPREAD instead of drilling (or sliding). The geometry self-classifies — a wide cliff has many mouths (spills fast), a crater pinhole has one (backs up, spreads) — no explicit hole-vs-edge test. Spring runtime (`water-spring-runtime.ts`) passes `?springdraincap` (default **1**) to both the seed-settle and live `flowStep`, and now sets the cull `floorY = origin.y − FLOW_DROP` (source-relative) instead of `minSolid − FLOW_DROP` (minSolid was the buried dirt-cone tip, ~48 voxels under the terrain, which let water drill to −75). Verified on the debug scan with the ACTUAL production `stepFluid`: vol/footprint 5.67→2.79 (flatter, spreads 80→127 wide), bottomY −75→−26 (bounded at the source floor). Locked into `tools/scan-siting/test.ts` (flow-throttle assertions: bounded, far shallower than unthrottled, flatter). Tune: `?springdraincap=N` (1 flattest), `?springflowdrop=N` (drop budget below source).
- **Full-relief basin band (Jul 19, v8):** Fixed the v7 basin finder mis-siting on real scans. The `BASIN_BAND` clip (`max(CEIL_BAND,8)` below `maxTop`) blinded `chooseBasin()` whenever `maxTop` is an outlier spike — on the debug scan `debugworld-21f243cd9379.glb` the median column-top is **1** but `maxTop` is **51** (a wall/ceiling fragment), so the 8-voxel skullcap saw no enclosure, returned null, and the spring fell back to sheeting off that spike (KJ's "water spawns and goes through geometry"). The obvious 0.55 m-deep crater in the terrain below was never considered. Fix: default `BASIN_BAND` to the **full top-surface relief** (`maxTop − minTop`, where `minTop` is the lowest column TOP, not lowest solid), still overridable via `?springbasinband`. The priority-flood already returns null on flat/monotone ceilings, so the wider search is safe. Verified offline against the debug GLB (faithful voxelizer harness: extracts the app's exact `WORKER_CODE` + runs the ported siting): now sites at natural-basin `(-29,1,-14)`, depth 37 (~0.55 m) on the real floor `y=1`, emitter cell air + open horizontal neighbours (over-lip, not through geometry), identical dry and raining. Saved springs below version 8 re-site once. Also removed a pre-existing `AMPLITUDE`/`FREQUENCY` ReferenceError in `report()`. Applies to production + staging (shared runtime).
- **Ceiling-aware basin band (Jul 19 pm):** The v8 full-relief band regressed on ROOFED scans. On the new ceiling scan `debugscan-dcd6e8c3a49d.glb` (60% of column tops sit at the ceiling ~179; ceiling GAPS drop to the floor ~0), the full-relief band let the watershed flood tunnel straight through a gap and report the whole floor-to-ceiling void (depth ~179) as the "deepest basin" — siting the spring on the FLOOR (`[-9,0,-70]`) instead of on the roof. Fix in `water-spring-runtime.ts` `init()`: detect a ceiling (`≥15%` of column tops within `CEIL_BAND` of `maxTop`) and cap `BASIN_BAND = min(relief, ?springceilband default 28)` so the basin is found ON the ceiling; open terrain (no ceiling — the old crater) keeps the full-relief band. Live + offline now site natural-basin `[28,158,35]` depth 21 on the roof; water pools up top (pool y 131→158) and no longer spawns on the floor. Answers KJ's "water should pool above the ceiling, not the floor." Mirrored in `tools/scan-siting/siting.mjs`; regression `test.ts` updated (containment now probed at a central FLOOR cell, independent of the ceiling-sited spring: raw-shell 15 → latent 0 below-floor columns). RESOLVED below — see "No mid-air water cull".
- **No mid-air water cull — water is conserved & falls to the floor (Jul 19 pm):** KJ's principle: *nothing in the design ever destroys water; a falling drop must reach the floor and pool, and a roof drip should hold at the lip a moment (surface tension/adhesion) then spill — never vanish.* The `origin.y − FLOW_DROP` **source-relative cull floor** (added when the debug scan was a hollow shell that let water drill to −75) rode UP with the spring once siting moved to the ceiling (origin y≈158 → cull line y≈132), so roof water was DELETED mid-air ~140 voxels above the real floor — that was the "doesn't run all the way down." Fix in `water-spring-runtime.ts` `init()`: removed the source-relative override; `floorY` stays anchored at `minSolid − FLOW_DROP` (BELOW the world's lowest solid). In an enclosed room the cull can never fire — the solid **latent underside** (the real "solid floor/walls" fix) is what stops the water now, not deletion; the only water that reaches this floor is genuine off-an-open-edge overflow that recirculates as the water cycle. The crest-dwell (`edgeHold`, default 5 ≈ 0.5s) already provides the "hold at the lip then spill" surface-tension read. Verified live: `floorY −38` (was ~132), `culledTotal 0`, pool spills off the ceiling basin and descends monotonically (poolY min 158→154→148→142→…) toward the floor with nothing culled. Offline `test.ts` flow block rewritten: asserts `culled === 0` (conservation in the sealed room) and pool bottom falls PAST the obsolete `origin.y − FLOW_DROP` line — GREEN (bottomY 74 < 132). NB the drain-cap (`maxDrainPerCol`, `?springdraincap` default 1) still flattens the descent; it only limits RATE, never destroys water.
- **Water sim is now latent-aware (Jul 20):** KJ: "invisible holes in the ground (especially dirt) — water streaming through the bottom." ROOT CAUSE (code-read): the spring's `isSolid` in `water-spring-runtime.ts` tested **colMap only** (drawn voxels) — the latent underside was solid to the camera guard (`visSolidAt`) but INVISIBLE to water, so wherever the visible crust had a gap (missing dirt/grass sample) water fell straight through the 875K-cell latent volume to the void. FIX: `createSpring` takes optional `extraSolid(x,y,z)`; the app passes `latentSolidAt` (spent-aware, so dug latent stays passable). Same fix applied to `settleWaterNear`'s local `isSolid` (edit-time scan-water re-pour). The offline latent oracle already predicted this (below-floor columns 15→0 with latent solid). Seam overlay also disabled by default same day (`?seam=1` re-enables; its ghost purple cubes at removed-floater columns came from reading stale ground-layer heights).
- **Airborne floater sweep at voxelize (Jul 19 pm):** The worker's tiny-clump prune only killed <3-vox specks, so larger disconnected clumps survived and read as floating cubes/spikes — floating grass+dirt patches (floorMap placed a floor on a scanned horizontal surface with nothing beneath; the wall-grounding pass only floods `objectVox`/`wall`, never grass/dirt) and hovering wall/ceiling fragments. Added a final all-layer 6-connected sweep in `tw-staging-core.tsx` `WORKER_CODE` (after tiny-clump, before visibility cull): KEEP a component only if it reaches the dominant floor `domY` (incl. dirt underside) or the dominant ceiling `domCeilY`; drop the rest. On the debug scan it drops ~50 ungrounded components (~687 vox). Distinct from the RUNTIME `groundSavedLayers` load pass in `tw-staging-app.tsx` (which anchors only to dirt/grass and SETTLES ceiling-hung walls down, leaving oversized comps in place) — the worker sweep is upstream and is the only thing that removes floating GRASS (an anchor layer the runtime never touches). Live `window.__tw.floaterReport().floatBlocks === 0` post-fix; wide screenshot shows no detached blocks in the sky. NB the reverted Jul-12 sweep was a per-LOAD runtime pass (froze load, ate trees); this one runs once at voxelize time with no trees in the preview worker.
- **Connectivity floater sweep (Jul 19 late) — THE fix for persistent floaters.** Diagnosis (live census via `window.__scene` walk + true 6-connectivity on rendered `slotMap`s): the ceiling scan shipped with **596 disconnected components (486 singles, ~1.8K vox)** — mauve wall/ceiling nubs + stray grass/dirt — yet the removal passes only touched ~130. Root cause: EVERY de-floater (worker airborne sweep's height test; runtime `surveyFloaters`/`groundSavedLayers`) tested "supported from below / at floor height," NEVER connectivity to the main mass. A stray floor sample sits at floor height (`groundMap>=y`) and a cull-orphaned nub often has a hidden voxel below → ~99% read as grounded and survived. Two generators: (a) `floorMap` mints a floating floor voxel from a lone elevated up-facing scan triangle; (b) the visibility cull (drops fully-occluded non-dirt voxels — only `dirt` keeps a `hidden_` bucket) runs AFTER the worker sweep, severing the interior bridges that held shell nubs to the mass. **Fix:** rewrote `surveyFloaters` in `tw-staging-app.tsx` to flood every `colMap` cell into 6-connected components, treat the LARGEST as the terrain (grounded), and flag every other component ≤`cap` as a floater (>`cap` kept as a real detached structure e.g. a ceiling sheet). Load call now `settleFloaters({deleteMax: DEFLOAT_CAP, cap: DEFLOAT_CAP})` with `DEFLOAT_CAP=1024` (junk runs ≤172; 1024 protects genuine geometry); `floaterReport` cap defaulted to 1024 to match. Veg/water/immovable exemptions + edge classification preserved; `groundRef`/`hiddenBelow` no longer consulted for float classification (still used by edit-time `settleIslandAt`). Live result: sweep deletes **1,932 blocks / 601 comps**, post-sweep census = **1 floater (the 2-vox water blob, intentionally exempt)**, main mass 197,441 unchanged. Verified `deleteMax:1024`, `bigGrounded:0`, no runtime errors. NB the worker sweep (note above) is now redundant belt-and-suspenders (runs pre-cull, can't see cull fragmentation) — the runtime connectivity sweep is authoritative. Upstream `floorMap` support-gate (reject unsupported up-facing samples) still an open option but not needed now that connectivity catches them.
- **Natural-basin siting (Jul 19, v7):** The spring now prefers a genuine EXISTING depression in the ceiling. `chooseBasin()` in `water-spring-runtime.ts` runs a priority-flood watershed (Barnes 2014) over the ceiling footprint (columns within `?springbasinband`, default `max(CEIL_BAND,8)`, of `maxTop`): each column's SPILL LEVEL = the height water must rise to before it escapes over the lowest surrounding rim, so `depth = spill − top`; it sites on the deepest enclosed column (rim strictly above it), tie-broken toward the surface centroid. A flat/unenclosed ceiling yields no basin → falls back to the v6 `chooseOutlet()` (nearest-central open-lip cell). It NEVER edits the scan — no carved bowl, no rim, no plinth (all removed in v6). Validated offline: picks the bowl bottom, rejects flat/open-slope ceilings, prefers the deeper of two bowls; on the real scanned debut world `w_mrr9eck2` it finds shallow (1–2 cell) real dips near the ceiling top. Saved springs below version 7 re-site once (`?springresite=1` forces it). Applies to production + staging (shared runtime).
- **Spring source contract corrected (Jul 19, v6):** A spring may select an existing cell in the highest ceiling band, preferably one with a genuine open horizontal lip, but it must not manufacture terrain. `water-spring-runtime.ts` no longer stamps surface variance, a basin floor, a plinth, or rim walls; its blue source marker only overlays the existing ceiling voxel and is not added to `colMap`. CA worlds also start with zero placed water instead of a load-time `fill(260)`, so water can reach a lower floor only by visibly travelling through air over the real ceiling edge. Saved springs below version 6 re-site once. This applies to production and staging because both import the shared runtime.

- **Rain-fed spring — habitat/irrigation engine (Jul 18)**: New `src/water-spring-runtime.ts` (composed from the pure, offline-tested `src/water-spring.ts` / `tools/water_spring.ts`; fixtures `tools/test_water_spring.ts`, 16/16) adds a spring that pools/flows and can irrigate. Design intent (KJ): water drives vegetation/fruit while the **strict conservation ledger stays** — two ledgers, and water never crosses into building matter (rain → finite spring budget → irrigation → food/greenery, not blocks). On load the runtime finds the highest solid surface (ceiling-top band, else terrain), stamps deterministic low-frequency `surfaceVariance` across it (the variance is *functional*: a flat surface clogs the emitter; the dips are the plumbing that lets each drop roll off), sites + stamps a visible spring block on the highest post-variance cell, and feeds a **capped** private spring-water mesh (cap = reservoir capacity; scene-only, NOT in `meshesRef`, so autosave/void/floater never touch it). Only `meta.spring {version, seed, origin, budget, capacity}` persists; geometry regenerates from seed each load (superTree pattern — never re-shifts). Two mesh findings that shaped it: `addLayer` sizes every mesh to exactly its cells (no spare water slots → dedicated capped mesh), and `snapshotLiveLayers` serializes any mesh with `layer`+`slotMap` (→ spring meshes keep neither). Rain uses the weather API's `current.precipitation`/`modifiers.isRaining` → `applyRain`; a throttled render-loop tick composes `springEmit`→`emitAndSettle` (the additive settle: targets `localVolume+N`, conservation-exact even for drips into a not-yet-connected pool, unlike edit-time `solveWaterFlow`). Knobs: `__tw.springReport()`, `__tw.rainNow(n)`, `__tw.springHere()`; off-switch `?spring=0`; tunables `?springamp/springfreq/springband/springcap/springraink/springtickms/springpertick`. Verified on the debug world: built clean (staging chunk `tinyworld-staging-Cw4wAN_K.js`), restarted, `springReport()` = active, sited `[57,17,22]`, emitter one above, capacity 900, primed 370-cell pool, budget draining (correct drought behavior — debug world isn't raining). **Visual wow (variance dips holding pools + blue spring block + edge waterfall) is a KJ device check** — headless debug shot showed the surface but not the transparent water/block clearly. **Irrigation tie shipped (Jul 18, chunk `tinyworld-staging-DWrrALOX.js`):** a throttled `runIrrigation()` unions scan-pond + spring `waterCells()`, dilates by `IRRIG_R` (default 3 — "a little further" than adjacent, `?irrigater`) with vertical reach `IRRIG_V` (default 4, `?irrigatev`), and marks watered soil columns. Two matter-neutral effects, no cells move: (1) grass greening — `collectGrassTops()` overrides the per-top `dry` flag (`effDry = dry && !isIrrigated`), so a `dryGrass` top within reach renders as lush blades on the next `markGrassDirty` rebuild (both full build and live rebuild go through `collectGrassTops`, so one injection covers both); (2) fruit gate — un-irrigated tree columns regrow fruit `FRUIT_DROUGHT_MULT`× slower (default 3, `?fruitdroughtmult`), recoverable not lethal per KJ. Off with `?irrigate=0`; gated on the spring being active, so worlds without it are unchanged. Knob `__tw.irrigationReport()`. Verified on the debug world: `irrigationReport()` = `{on:true, irrigated:120, R:3, V:4, fruitDroughtMult:3}`, spring still active, no console errors. **Visible dry→green flip is a KJ device check** — the debug world's grass is already lush (little dryGrass to flip), so a summer scan with brown grass near water is needed to see it. **Arid grass shipped (Jul 18, chunk `tinyworld-staging-C4yHJkCq.js`):** the inverse of irrigation — a soil column farther than `ARID_R` (Chebyshev, default 9, `?aridr`; must exceed `IRRIG_R`) from surface water is "arid". `runIrrigation()` now also dilates water by `ARID_R` to get the hydrated band and flags every soil column outside it. Two matter-neutral effects, no cells move: (1) blades — `collectGrassTops()` forces `dry=true` for arid tops so they render the dry palette (yellow); (2) block — the ground grass/dryGrass `InstancedMesh` gets a per-instance `instanceColor` (lazily allocated, white=no-tint, one material recompile) and arid blocks are tinted toward `PAL.dryGrass` via a ratio computed from the mesh's actual base color (fall-season safe). Only changed columns re-tint + re-blade per pass. Gated on `aridEnabled` (`?arid=0` off) AND `irrigationOn`, so a world with no spring/pond never goes all-arid. Knob `__tw.aridReport()`. This creates the intended oasis read: green ring near water → normal → arid yellow beyond. Verified on the debug world: `aridReport()` = `{on:true, arid:8653, R:9}`, `irrigationReport()` 120 irrigated, spring active, no console errors, and the agent-browser shot clearly shows the yellow field with a green patch by the spring (both block + blades yellow). Exact hue on device is a KJ check. **Arid gradient — de-stark (Jul 18, chunk `tinyworld-staging-cmW2rDZF.js`):** KJ found the binary flip too stark (green ring, then a hard yellow wall past R=9). Aridity now RAMPS with distance: `runIrrigation()` computes each soil column's min Chebyshev distance to the nearest water column (collapsed to unique x,z tops so deep pools stay cheap), then maps distance through a smoothstep from `ARID_NEAR` (dryness 0, `?aridnear`, default `IRRIG_R+1`=4; `?aridr` is a back-compat alias for the ramp start) to `ARID_FAR` (dryness 1, `?aridfar`, default 220 — walked 22→60→220 on Jul 18 as KJ asked for progressively softer fades ("3× longer", then "10× further"); at 220 across the ~60–80-cell debug tile nothing reaches full arid, so it reads as an almost-all-green world with a whisper of drying at the edges. KJ LOCKED THIS IN Jul 18 ("10x is the right approach")); columns with no water within `ARID_FAR` (or none within `IRRIG_V` vertically) are fully arid. The distance field iterates soil×water (not a per-water dilation radius) so cost is independent of `ARID_FAR` — no frame hitch at large ranges. **Moisture model + rain (Jul 18, chunk `tinyworld-staging-CmT11Oze.js`):** KJ asked for the update to ease (not snap) AND for rain to wet vegetation — green that holds for a while then decays to yellow. Both are one mechanism now. Each grass column's DISPLAYED dryness (`aridCols`, still read by `collectGrassTops` + block tint) EASES toward a target instead of jumping. Target = `1 - max(waterMoist, rainMoist)`: `waterMoist = 1 - distanceDryness` (the permanent oasis floor), `rainMoist = 1` while within `GREEN_HOLD_MS` of the last rain else `0`. So rain greens the whole field, holds `GREEN_HOLD_MS`, then far-from-water columns decay back to the oasis gradient. Two cadences: HEAVY `recomputeMoisture()` every `IRRIG_MS` (`?irrigms`, default 8000) rebuilds the cached distance field + irrigation set + top-soil-block slots; LIGHT `easeMoisture()` every ~1s steps each column toward target — drying slow (`?decayms`, default 22000), greening-after-rain faster (`?greenms`, default 6000) — re-tints the block (cheap per-instance color) every step and re-blades only when the column crosses a coarse 1/4 bucket (bounds rebuilds). Rain source: real weather `modifiers.isRaining` sets `lastGrassRainMs` each frame; manual demo `__tw.wetGrass()` (returns the timings). The hold is a REAL-TIME window: **default 2 DAYS** (KJ Jul 18 — "green should be much longer, 2 days"), tracked in wall-clock `Date.now()` (NOT `performance.now()`, which resets each load) and PERSISTED as `meta.grassLastRainMs`, so grass a player rained on stays green across sessions until the 2 days truly elapse (restored on build via `recordRain()`/meta init). Knobs: `?greenholdh` (hours) or `?greenhold` (ms). `__tw.aridReport()` includes `rainHeldMs` (ms left of hold). Verified (chunk `tinyworld-staging-nTuFjhsA.js`): `wetGrass()` → `greenHoldMs=172800000` (2.00 days), field greens (`full` 6580→0) and STAYS green across samples (the 90s hold used to revert between slow evals; now it holds), no errors. Verified headless (far=30 debug world): `wetGrass()` → whole field greens (`full` 6580→0), `rainHeldMs`≈hold, before/after agent-browser shots show yellow→all-green, no errors; the hold/decay math is correct (atomic probe read `held`=7999 right after wetGrass) — cross-call timing tests are unreliable because each agent-browser eval burns ~4–11s of page time (`performance.now()` advanced 11.6s during a 3s sleep, `__tw.wetGrass` identity stable so NOT a rebuild). NOTE at the locked `far=220` the world is near-all-green so rain/decay has little dry range to show — the effect is subtle at that setting; a smaller `?aridfar` gives more yellow for rain to green. Easing/decay feel on device is a KJ check. `aridCols` is now `Map<col,dryness>` (quantized to 1/12 to avoid re-tint churn). `setBlockArid(mesh, slot, dryness)` LERPs the per-instance block color white→full-arid by dryness (partial = faded), and `collectGrassTops()` stipples green blades to dry with probability = dryness via a stable per-column `aridHash01`, so the blade edge dithers across the gradient instead of snapping on a circle. Knob `__tw.aridReport()` now returns `{on, arid, full, near, far}`. Verified on the debug world: `aridReport()` = `{on:true, arid:8875, full:7741, near:4, far:22}` (≈1134-column gradient ring, vs the old all-or-nothing), no console errors, and the agent-browser shot shows a smooth green→yellow fade out from the spring (full arid only at the far edges). Exact look on device is a KJ check. **Water flow is a static resting-level solve, NOT an animated waterfall** — `solveWaterFlow`/`emitAndSettle` place water in the lowest *supported* cells (rests on solid or a filled cell below), filling bottom-up, so a drop over a ledge fills the base pool up rather than hanging as a falling column. You get pooling + tiered overflow/spill to lower basins (reads as "flowed downhill"), not a Minecraft-style flowing waterfall down a wall. `chooseSpringOrigin`'s "overflows the edge as a waterfall" comment is aspirational; a real falling-water visual would be a separate render feature. **Pool-invisible bug FIXED + waterfall v1 (Jul 18, chunk `tinyworld-staging-yIC4iGLm.js`):** found the reason the spring "water wow" never showed — `water-spring-runtime.ts` set `waterMesh.count = 0` at creation and `reconcile()` never raised it, so the pool rendered ZERO instances (invisible) the whole time. `reconcile()` now sets `waterMesh.count = maxUsedSlot+1` → the pool actually renders (confirmed: blue translucent voxels visible in agent-browser shot). Added a **waterfall overlay** (matter-neutral, NOT in springWater/colMap/ledger — pure render, so irrigation/autosave/budget untouched): `renderWaterfalls()` scans the pool for "lip" cells (a water cell whose horizontal neighbour is air at pool level AND air directly below = a drop) and fills a capped `waterfallMesh` with a falling column down that neighbour to the first solid/water below (or `?waterfalldrop` cells, default 30, into the void for an island-edge overflow); called from `fill()` after every reconcile. Knobs: `?waterfall=0` off, `?waterfalldemo=1` floods the reservoir (`state.budget=CAPACITY`, `fill(CAPACITY)`, wider `?springradius` default 64, flatter `?springrise` default 4 in demo) so the pool overflows and spawns falls, `__tw.springReport().waterfallCells`. Verified headless: demo → pool 428 cells, **391 waterfall cells**, 0 errors, blue water clearly visible in the shot. **HONEST STATUS / KJ device call:** the water is now VISIBLE and the overflow mechanism works, but the debug-world look is a chunky voxel water BLOB near the spring, not a dramatic cascade — the pool doesn't reach the island rim there so the falls are short local drops, and the cells are full opaque-ish voxels (no animated flow yet). A beautiful cascade (thin/animated water, guaranteed off-the-island-edge fall, splash) is v2 and needs KJ's aesthetic direction. Static v1 chosen over an animated shader because transparent-water look can't be verified in headless Chromium. **Water "stacking up" fix (Jul 18, chunk `tinyworld-staging-ClS5v5Oi.js`):** once the pool became visible, KJ saw it as a tall vertical WALL of water. Root causes (bbox proved it: pool was 11×15 footprint but 5 cells TALL, perched at y16-20): (1) `chooseSpringOrigin` sited the spring on the HIGHEST surface cell — in the debug world that's the TABLE — so water heaped on a high spot with no basin to pool into; (2) `maxRise` was 12 (→ tall pile); (3) the waterfall over-triggered on the pool's vertical FACES (every side cell was a "lip") making a curtain. Fixes: `chooseSpringOrigin(topCells, variance, {preferLow})` now defaults to siting in the LOWEST central dip (`?springpeak=1` for the old high siting) so water pools flat into a basin as a lake; `MAX_RISE` default 2 (`?springrise`); waterfall lips are SURFACE-only (skip any pool cell with water above it). Existing worlds relocate their perched spring ONCE via a `meta.spring.version` bump (2 → re-site if saved `<2`; `?springresite=1` forces it). Verified on the debug world (no flag): spring auto-relocated `[57,17,22]`→`[58,14,9]`, pool bbox now 12×10 footprint × **3 tall** (was 5), 135 cells, 0 errors, and the shot shows a low flat pool instead of the wall. `__tw.springReport().poolBBox` added for diagnosis. Cascade polish (animated/thin water, guaranteed off-edge fall, splash) still a KJ-directed v2. **Spread-outward flood — never pulls straight up (Jul 18, chunk `tinyworld-staging-SL5KP2CD.js`):** KJ's follow-up — "water should never pull straight upward; when there's no lower surface it should propagate outward uniformly… spread outwards until it hits real terrain walls." Root cause of the residual upward stacking: `emitAndSettle`'s pocket flood was a plain FIFO breadth-first fill bounded by a fixed radius box + `maxCells`, so it captured a narrow compact blob (incl. vertical air) around the emitter; once that narrow low layer filled, leftover volume climbed inside the invisible box edge. Rewrote the flood as a **spill-level priority flood (Barnes 2014)**: each reachable cell gets a SPILL LEVEL = the highest y water must cross to reach it from the source (flat/downhill ⇒ source height; behind a wall ⇒ the wall's rim height), computed via a min-heap keyed on `L` then distance (marking seen at push is exact because the min-heap pops each cell first at its minimal L). Water then fills in `(level, then y, then distance)` order. Consequences, all proven by offline fixtures (`test_flood.ts`, 3/3): (1) OPEN FLAT GROUND → a 1-cell-thick sheet that spreads outward, never towers (add 400 → footprint 400, maxStack 1, ySpread 0), regardless of the rise ceiling; (2) REAL BASIN → fills bottom-up INSIDE the solid walls and can't teleport over a wall into lower outside ground it hasn't yet risen to reach (add 60 into a 25-cell walled bowl → footprint 25, 3 deep, all inside); (3) STEP-DOWN CLIFF → flows down to fill the genuinely-lower connected ground first. Real solid terrain is now the only wall — no invisible box dam. With stacking impossible on open ground at any ceiling, `MAX_RISE` (`?springrise`) was raised 2 → 6 (demo 3 → 8) so genuine basins can fill to their real rim before overflowing the lip; the priority flood guarantees open ground still stays flat. Verified on the debug world (no flag): `springReport().poolBBox` = 11×13 footprint, water settled DOWN from emitter y15 into the basin at y3–9 (flowing downhill, not piling up), 418 cells, 136 waterfall lip-cells (spilling over the rim), 0 errors. The debug tile's spring low-point is a genuine ~7-deep depression so it pools there and waterfalls over — correct physics, but the transparent-water LOOK on device is a KJ check (single headless angle + translucent water can't conclusively prove "flat/non-stacking"; the ALGORITHM is proven by the fixtures). **Cellular-automata flow rewrite (Jul 18, chunk `tinyworld-staging-BMMM0LUV.js`):** KJ rejected the instant resting-solve entirely after it kept "stacking" — the one-shot `emitAndSettle` filled the ~900 NEAREST cells then piled leftover volume upward (an invisible cell-budget wall, not terrain). He asked for a real CA model ("water should tick downward from the source before hitting ground; spread outward until it hits real terrain walls") and best-in-class references. Referenced: W-Shadow mass-CA + "Game of Flow" (horizontal equalization = why grid water self-levels and never towers), Timberborn (height-field sheet over terrain + pressure — best stylized voxel water), Minecraft (7-step discrete flow + source blocks), Dwarf Fortress (7-level pressure flow), Noita/falling-sand (down→diagonal→sideways priority). Built a discrete falling-sand CA on the EXISTING binary voxel grid (keeps render/conservation/irrigation intact — chose this over a mass/height-field rewrite which would change render+persistence): new pure `stepFluid(water, isSolid, opts)` in `src/water-spring.ts`, advancing the fluid ONE cell of motion per call. Rules per water cell (processed lowest-y first, direction order rotates with `tick` to kill bias): (1) FALL if below free; (2) SLIDE down-diagonal if straight-down blocked; (3) SPREAD via Minecraft-style bounded DOWNHILL-SEEKING — a bounded BFS (`flowDir`, `spreadRange` default 16, `maxScan` 200) over the reachable same-level supported surface for the nearest "drop" (a position water could descend from — lower/unfilled column, basin rim, or void edge), step one cell toward it; NO drop in reach ⇒ the cell RESTS. That downhill guard is the key: a thin sheet on flat ground doesn't creep/jitter, water always flows toward edges, and piles self-level (an unfilled neighbour column reads as a drop). Cells that fall below `floorY` (= lowest terrain − `?springflowdrop` 26) are CULLED and their volume REFUNDED to the rain-fed reservoir (conservation-exact water cycle: reservoir→spring→pool→overflow→falls→recirculate). Runtime (`water-spring-runtime.ts`): `?cawater=1` default (`=0` reverts to the legacy instant solve); `tick()` now DRIBBLES one source cell at the emitter every `?springemitms` (140ms) and advances `flowStep()` every `?springflowms` (90ms); `prime(seed)` fast-forwards the same CA at load (bounded to `?springseedcap` 220 + 320 settle iters so a big budget can't freeze load) so the initial pool is already flat (no tower on load); the old matter-neutral waterfall overlay is SKIPPED under CA (real pool water now falls as real cells). Proven by 6 new offline fixtures (`test_step_fluid.ts` in the con workspace, 11/11): descends exactly 1 cell/step; an 8-tall tower on flat ground self-levels to a 1-deep sheet (maxY=1, never towers) and SETTLES finitely; a walled basin fills bottom-up contained; overflow spills over the low lip and drains off the edge. Verified on the live served bundle (debug world): `springReport().ca=true`, and time-sampling showed water TICKING DOWNWARD (pool bbox ymin descending 3→1→−1→−3→−5→−7 as it pours off and free-falls toward floorY=−26) while ymax stayed pinned at the source y15 (NO upward stacking — the old bug is gone) and stepCount advanced (animated, not instant). On the debug world's perched-spring/flat-ground geometry water drains off as a thin stream rather than forming a deep lake — physically correct (no basin there); a real basin world pools. `springReport()` gains `{ca, floorY, stepCount, culledTotal}`. **KJ device check:** transparent thin/falling water doesn't render legibly in headless Chromium (top-down shot didn't clearly show the stream, which falls off-island into the void behind the tile) — the BEHAVIOR is proven by fixtures+telemetry but the LOOK needs KJ on device; and per-cell BFS is a mobile perf watch (tunable via `?springscan`/`?springspread`/`?springflowms`). Smooth animated water SURFACE render (height-field over the sim, the Timberborn/shader look) is the natural next step and was NOT done here — this milestone is flow BEHAVIOR only. **Varied-terrain test world + SITING root cause (Jul 18, chunk `tinyworld-staging-CFXoKDb0`→lean rebuild):** KJ asked to (1) add 4 springs, (2) quarter the water voxel size, (3) enlarge/vary the debug terrain to test pooling. Built #3 — `makeWaterTestWorldSource()` in `tw-staging-app.tsx`, a full-grid island (basins, a shallow side basin, a valley channel, a cliff plateau, a low stepped shore) behind `?debugWorld=1&terrain=hydro`; it RENDERS correctly (agent-browser shot shows the varied terrain, plateau, basins). BUT it surfaced THE deeper root cause behind every "water perches on the highest thing" complaint: `water-spring-runtime.ts:172` defines the spring's usable `surface` as ONLY `tops.filter(c => c[1] >= maxTop - CEIL_BAND)` — i.e. the top ~3-cell band of the ENTIRE world. On flat/near-flat worlds that's everything (fine); on varied terrain it's only the plateau, so `chooseSpringOrigin(surface, …)` can only site on the highest feature and literally cannot see the low basins. That (not the CA flow, which is fixed) is why the spring kept perching on the table/high ground. On the hydro world the spring never attached `springReport` in headless checks (either sited on the plateau or bailed "no usable surface" — inconclusive headless, known agent-browser flakiness). **The priority fix is spring siting: build `surface` from the FULL terrain (or at least allow low-basin siting), not just the top band — this unlocks the varied test world AND fixes the live-game perch-stacking.** NOT yet done (touches live-world siting → wants care + KJ device confirm). Recommendations given to KJ: (a) #2 quarter-voxel water — DON'T; water shares the terrain grid so quartering = 16–64× cells → iOS GPU/VRAM risk + breaks conservation/irrigation; the chunky look is a RENDER problem → smooth animated water SURFACE over the coarse grid is the real fix; half-res is the max to risk and only after the surface render; (b) #1 four springs — good stress test but a refactor (system is single-emitter; do one controller w/ N emitters feeding a shared pool, not 4 fighting instances), do it on the varied world after the siting fix; (c) recommended order: siting fix → varied world becomes the real test bed → 4-spring test → smooth water surface; skip quarter-voxel sim. **Siting fix SHIPPED + basin-fill fix (Jul 18, chunk `tinyworld-staging-Chz2vQwO`):** KJ opened the hydro world, saw no water. Two compounding bugs found+fixed in `water-spring-runtime.ts`: (1) **spring bailed "no usable surface"** — a tree sitting on the plateau made `maxTop`≈21, so the top-band `surface` (within CEIL_BAND=3 of maxTop) held only a few leaf cells (<8) → bail. Fix: the sanity check now uses `tops.length` (full terrain) not the fragile band; siting now passes the FULL `tops` to `chooseSpringOrigin` (not just top-band `surface`) so `preferLow` finds the real basin floor. Version bumped 2→3 so persisted worlds re-site once. Trees moved off the plateau. (2) **point source can't spread on a flat basin floor** — the CA's spread rule (`flowDir`) only moves water toward a STRICT drop (anti-jitter design); a spring at the flat bottom of a bowl has no reachable drop → water rests as ONE cell, basin never fills. Fix: seed the initial pool with `fill()` (=`emitAndSettle`, the resting-level priority flood proven to fill basins bottom-up in `test_flood.ts`) instead of the CA point-dribble `prime()`; default `?springseed` 48→260 so it's a visible lake. Live CA still animates overflow/spill. Verified on hydro world: spring `origin [32,4,37]` (central basin, was perching/bailing), pool = **260 cells, 18×17 footprint, 3 deep** (`poolBBox` y5–7), culled 0. Translucent water faint in headless top-down shot (known limitation) — LOOK is KJ device check, data proves the lake. **REMAINING CA GAP (next focused step):** the live source still can't grow a lake from a point on FLAT ground (needs a self-leveling / pressure-spread rule — spread sideways when a cell has water above it — with a jitter-safe tie-break; the doc comment at `water-spring.ts:253` describes this "piled ≥2-deep spread" but the code was replaced by downhill-only seeking). Until then, basins are SEEDED as resting lakes at load, not grown by the trickle. Hydro test world default is `?debugWorld=1&terrain=hydro` (N=72: central basin=primary lake, side basin, valley notch→west-edge cascade, cliff plateau, plain). **Waterlogged grass + 4× output (Jul 18/19, chunk `tinyworld-staging-CXLKjGYi.js`):** KJ's two follow-ups off the hydro lake shot. (1) **4× water output** for a denser overflow/waterfall — runtime defaults bumped `springcap` 900→3600, `springpertick` 2→8, `springraink` 8→32 (cadences unchanged). More cells in the falling column = thicker fall, gated by the cap (raised) + rain budget. A *solid/smooth* cascade is still the deferred v2 surface render — output alone can't beat coarse-voxel chunkiness. (2) **Waterlogged grass** — a third moisture state completing the symmetry `arid(dry)↔lush↔waterlogged(too wet)`: a grass top with a water cell directly ABOVE it (`waterTopMap > soilTop`) for longer than `WATERLOG_HOLD_MS` (default 60s, `?waterloghold` ms / `?waterloghours`, `?waterlog=0` off) de-lushes to a short sparse tuft on a plain GREEN tile (recoverable the instant water recedes). Matter-neutral, no cells move: detection/timing lives in `recomputeMoisture` (new `submergeSince` map + `waterloggedCols` set), render in `genBladesForTop` (wet ⇒ ~28% blade count, ×0.4 height) via a `wet` flag threaded through `collectGrassTops` (wet forces `dry=false` so it stays green not yellow, and re-blades on state flip via `markGrassDirty`). Knobs `__tw.waterlogReport()`, `__tw.drownGrass()` (force-flip now, skips hold). Verified on hydro world: spring active w/ 4× live (`cap:3600, perTick:8`), `waterlogReport` detected **213 columns submerging**, `drownGrass()` flipped **261 → waterlogged**, 0 console errors, scene renders clean. Short-tuft LOOK + fall density on device = KJ check. **Cascade polish — crest-hold + droplet split (Jul 19, chunk `tinyworld-staging-DTY-4A_I.js`):** KJ's two waterfall asks off the overflow shot. (1) **Edge crest-hold** — a cell perched at a spill lip now dwells `?springedgehold` steps (default 5 ≈ 0.5s at FLOW_MS 90) before pouring, so water bunches at the rim and holds the pool behind a touch fuller instead of draining the instant it reaches the edge. Implemented as an opt-in rule "0" in the pure `stepFluid` (new `edgeHold`/`dwell` opts; `edgeHold=0` is a proven byte-identical no-op so old fixtures are unaffected): a cell qualifies only if it would spill over a GENUINE ledge (straight-down OR a down-diagonal where the target AND the cell below it are both free → water will free-fall, not a 1-step downhill slide) AND has same-level pool water behind it AND is uncovered; it holds in place, blocking the feed, until its per-cell counter hits 0. The diagonal-SLIDE case matters: on a sharp lip water takes the down-diagonal over the edge (rule 2) and never rests at pool level, so the dwell HAD to intercept before both FALL and SLIDE (first attempt only guarded FALL → never fired). `dwell` is a runtime-owned Map pruned each step to live cells. (2) **Falling cells split into droplets** — render-only, CA-only, matter-neutral: `reconcile()` now classifies each body cell as resting-pool (nothing solid/water directly below → full voxel in `waterMesh`) vs FALLING (renders as `?springsubvox` default 4 small `?springdropscale` 0.42 jittered sub-voxels in a new private `dropMesh`); the moment a falling cell lands it reclassifies to pool and draws as a full voxel = "reforms at the bottom". `springWater` stays the authoritative full body (pool+falling) so irrigation/`waterCells`/conservation are untouched; the legacy waterfall overlay stays skipped under CA. Droplet offsets are a stable per-(x,z,i) hash so a vertical stream reads as coherent descending lanes, not flicker. `?springsplit=0` off. Proven by 6 offline fixtures (`test_edge_hold.ts` in con workspace, 6/6): crest holds MORE water on the ledge early (4 vs 3), still fully drains, `edgeHold=0` identical to no-option, dwell pruned, walled basin still conserves + self-levels with hold on. Verified live on the hydro demo (`?debugWorld=1&terrain=hydro&waterfalldemo=1`): `split:true, edgeHold:5`, `fallingCells` climbing 0→4→9→12 (droplet spray active), `dwelling` 6→3→10→9 (crest holding at lips), `waterfall:0` (overlay correctly skipped), 0 console errors. `springReport()` gains `{split, edgeHold, fallingCells, dwelling}`. Transparent water + tiny droplets don't render legibly in headless Chromium (top-down loading shot inconclusive) — the LOOK/feel of the cascade is a KJ device check; behavior proven by fixtures+telemetry. Smooth animated water SURFACE render is still the deferred v2. **"Pools less now" — NOT a regression + demo pooling fix (Jul 19, chunk `index-ByhUTL37.js`):** KJ compared two shots ("pooling more is the old version"). Root cause: the two shots differed only by the `?waterfalldemo=1` flag, NOT old-vs-new build. Measured proof: the NORMAL view (`?debugWorld=1&terrain=hydro`, no demo) still pools a 20×18×3 lake (300 cells) — identical to the "pooling more" shot; crest-hold+droplet physics is unchanged (confirmed A/B: new-defaults 284→356 vs `?springedgehold=0&springsplit=0` 300→364, same fill rate). The "pools less" shot was the DEMO, which by design floods wide+flat (`springradius` 64, `fill(CAPACITY)`=3600 dumped) to force an overflow → sheets thin and drains as a channel. Since KJ had been handed the demo link to inspect the waterfall, he was judging the wrong view. FIRST FIX ATTEMPT (WRONG, reverted same turn, chunk `index-ByhUTL37.js`): narrowed the demo `FILL_RADIUS` 64→30 to "pool more" → gave `pool≈1900, 47×44`. KJ correctly rejected it: **`FILL_RADIUS` is a PERF bound on the flood search, NOT a basin-size design knob — using it to confine water artificially designs the lake, which he explicitly does not want.** The lake size must be EMERGENT: the terrain's real basins (priority flood fills lowest-first, bounded by actual solid walls) decide where/how deep water pools; the flow rate decides how much arrives. The normal 300-cell/3-deep lake already proves this is emergent (it's the terrain's basin, not a set number). The thin-sheet "pools less" demo was simply the reservoir DUMP (`fill(CAPACITY)`=3600 ≈ 12× the ~300-cell basin capacity) correctly overflowing and spreading thin — real water behavior, not a regression. CORRECT FIX (chunk `index-BE0MHV9F.js`): reverted `FILL_RADIUS` to a single generous perf bound (24, no demo special) and removed the demo reservoir dump — the demo is now just "infinite rain" (`state.budget = CAPACITY` so water never runs dry), seeding the same 260 as normal and letting the terrain + rate shape the lake. Verified: demo `pool=300, bbox 20×3×18` (identical to the normal view — same emergent basin) then grows by the flow rate (300→372, footprint 20→29 spreading to connected lower ground, depth pinned at the basin's 3). Deeper/bigger lakes come ONLY from deeper terrain basins (a scan/terrain property) + siting the spring in them (the known open siting fix), never a radius/seed knob. Separately, the droplet split (falling cells → 0.42-scale sub-voxels) makes over-edge water look thinner/quicker than the old full-voxel fall — if KJ wants fuller falls the levers are `?springdropscale` (0.42→~0.62) / `?springsubvox` (4→6) / `?springsplit=0`; NOT changed as a default yet (pending KJ pick). **Ceiling-siting fix (Jul 19, chunk `tinyworld-staging-DCfWrRV9.js`):** the varied-terrain milestone had made default siting pull from the FULL `tops` set with `preferLow`, which picked the world's GLOBAL low point (the floor) — so on real ceiling worlds the spring spawned way down low. KJ: "water spawned in a very lower location — needs to be on/above the ceiling." Fixed `water-spring-runtime.ts` to always site from `surface` (the top `CEIL_BAND` band = highest solid surface / ceiling), with `preferLow` now choosing a low DIP *within that ceiling band* (variance-carved) so water still pools instead of perching on the highest bump. Bumped `meta.spring.version` 3→4 and RESITE threshold `<4` so existing worlds relocate their floor-sited spring UP onto the ceiling once on next load (`?springresite=1` forces it; `?springpeak=1` reverts to high-bump siting). Verified on the debug world: `springReport().origin` = `[58,14,9]` (top band, was pulling to floor), active, no errors. True ceiling-pool look on a real scanned room is a KJ device check (debug world's highest feature is a table, not a ceiling; transparent water doesn't render legibly headless). **Ceiling water was INVISIBLE → carved-basin fix (Jul 19, chunk `tinyworld-staging-D1LBJp7w.js`):** after ceiling-siting, KJ: "I don't seem to see the water spawn at all." Root cause: `surfaceVariance` only adds random BUMPS (raises columns 0..amplitude), never a containing rim — so on a flat ceiling/roof slab the emitted water sheets off the edge and falls away → nothing visible on top. Confirmed on debug: emitter at y15 but pool landed at y3–4 (floor), water having fallen off the high surface. Fix: after siting, `water-spring-runtime.ts` now CARVES A BASIN around the origin — for every column within `?springbasin` (default 5) Chebyshev radius, flatten the interior UP to the spring's base height (`floorY0`) and raise a ring wall to `floorY0 + ?springrim` (default 4). Critically the bowl is founded on EACH column's own solid top via a `topByKey` map built from the FULL `tops` set (not just the top CEIL_BAND band) so the floor has NO holes for water to fall through (first attempt built the bowl from `surface`-band columns only → on the small debug table most in-radius columns are floor/excluded → holes → water fell to y3 again). On a true ceiling slab the fill is near-flat; on lower terrain it's a solid plinth. Overflow spills over the rim as a fall. `?springbasin=0` disables the bowl. Version bumped 3→5 (RESITE `<5`) so saved worlds re-site onto the ceiling AND get the basin once. Verified on the debug world: pool now at **y15–18** (up at the spring, was y3–4), 9×9 footprint, `culledTotal:0` (contained, nothing draining off) — proving water pools on top in the bowl instead of sheeting to the floor. The rooftop-pond LOOK on a real scanned room (proper dense ceiling) is a KJ device check; the debug "table" is only an approximate ceiling proxy and headless transparent water reads faintly.

- **Canonical floater order + bounded water pooling (Jul 18)**: Non-metric canonical GLB conversion now completes structural dedupe and `prune_floating()` before `decorate_vegetation()`, so unsupported scan wall/stone islands are removed before regular honey-locust trunks/leaves exist. Saved-world runtime vegetation protection remains required because those payloads already contain authored trees. Staging replaces the old local lowest-cell settle with `src/water-flow.ts`: a deterministic, volume-preserving solve of the connected water body nearest an edit, bounded to radius/cell limits, filling supported lowest cells first so water falls, spreads, pools, and stacks without crossing solids or exchanging with disconnected/remote ponds. Regression fixtures live in `tools/test_voxel_floater_order.py` and `tools/test_water_flow.ts`; both pass, Vite builds, Zo Space has no route errors, and Zo Browser loads the debug world. Visual edit-loop behavior on a real device remains a device playtest.

- **Honey-locust silhouette + tree grounding fix (Jul 14)**: `environmental-nyc-v1` no longer grows every primary limb from one shared trunk-top point. Honey-locust limbs now emerge at staggered heights, ascend with varied rise, split into irregular airy foliage lobes, and retain small crown perforations; this removes the clipped/flat tabletop silhouette while preserving deterministic species identity. The live staging `groundSavedLayers()` structural settle path now excludes `trunks` and `leaves` entirely (`wall`/`ceiling`/`stone`/`block` only), so deliberately disconnected procedural branches and canopy lobes cannot be mistaken for debris and dropped onto terrain.
