Generator changes require regeneration/re-upload from the source GLB; the grounding protection applies on the next load.

- **Single GLB tree path (Jul 14)**: Direct staging/production GLB uploads no longer author regular trees in their browser workers. Those workers are persistence-first, treeless previews only. `/api/tinyworld-upload-glb` starts `tools/canonical_glb_world.py`, which runs `tools/glb_to_world_payload.py` with `VOXEL_M=auto` / `TARGET_DIVS=690`, replaces the preview payload through the backed-up `update_world_from_payload` path, and exposes `canonical_running|canonical_done|canonical_error`; staging reloads on completion. The removed browser builder was the recurring source of short single-column trunks and spherical leaf blobs after a world clear. Regular trees are now authored only by `environmental-nyc-v1`. The runtime may append the super tree only to a canonical payload (`treesUpgraded:true`), never to the browser preview. Super-tree siting converts root-court radius, shaft clearance, wall distance, headroom, flatness, and preferred rise from meters through the payload voxel size; its chosen origin remains in `meta.superTree` and is included in the next autosave. Do not restore `MAX_TREES`, `treeCandidates`, `chosenTrees`, or browser-side trunk/canopy loops.

- **Tree-bound fruit + physical produce larder (Jul 13)**: Staging no longer spawns fruit from random leaf samples or forces it to fall to the ground after 18 seconds. Fruit now uses a bounded deterministic set of canopy sockets, each in the voxel directly beneath a real leaf; the smaller fruit geometry is lifted within that cell so it visibly touches the leaf. It stays attached until harvested, then the empty socket receives a deterministic season-scaled 3–6 minute regrowth deadline. Old saved ground fruit is reattached to available tree sockets once on load. Workers retain the physical `WORLD → CARRIED → STOCKPILE` ferry path. The depot is now a raised pallet plus an open wooden produce crate: fruit/seed/nectar render one-for-one inside the crate while bulk building matter stays on the pallet. `__tw.fruitReport()` exposes sockets, growing/attached/detached counts, regrowth waits, and larder stored/shown counts; `fruitGrowNow(n)` is the deterministic debug trigger. Build passed; runtime debug-world test grew 3 fruit with `attached:3/detached:0`, moved 2 into the larder with `stored:2/shown:2`, and kept the ledger balanced with zero world drift. Zo Browser loaded the new debug route and worker HUD; its subsequent interaction agent stalled, so detailed counters were verified through the local runtime harness.

- **Worker knowledge + bounded experimentation v2 (Jul 13)**: `src/tinyworld-learning.ts` and `docs/worker-knowledge-and-experimentation.md` implement the deterministic figure-it-out loop in staging. Workers persist beliefs, skills, outcomes, lessons, preferences, and at most one pending experiment. Each technique now gets an inspected baseline build before a candidate; experiments alternate with baseline practice, accept only when the candidate beats the base technique's running average, and record move/targeted issue/baseline samples/score delta/decision. Recent inspection issues bias the next bounded move (roof→roof, access→door, room/foundation→dimensions, incomplete execution→simplify), with deterministic fallbacks that avoid no-op variations. Workers announce both the hypothesis and keep/revert result in world-space speech. Nearby teaching, settlement-library codification, player evidence through `cityTeach`, `__tw.knowledgeReport(id?)`, `__tw.libraryReport()`, and `cityReport()` remain active. Deterministic tests verified a roof candidate beating 0.72 by +0.08 and a doorway candidate losing against 0.81 by −0.11; Vite built clean, staging restarted, served bundle `tinyworld-staging-BznDurzj.js`, and Zo Browser loaded the live debug-world HUD/worker. Zo Browser's Structures interaction stalled, so a complete baseline→experiment speech-bubble cycle remains a live playtest rather than claimed visual proof. Deferred: distinct joint-work/observation transfer, worker death/knowledge loss, physical workshop/library gates, offline semantic-event practice, rare LLM reflection/notification summaries, and full natural-language questioning about remembered experiments.
- **Reference-derived staged construction grammar (Jul 13)**: The persistent city-construction loop now derives its six families from `docs/building-reference-portfolio.md` instead of using generic boxes. Techniques carry developmental generation (1 shelter → 5 civic), reference ID, floor count, 2–5 façade bays, and 0–3 deterministic optional moves. New defaults are worker-scale and materially structural: stone foundation → stone load-bearing frame/supported upper floors → compacted-earth wall infill with a real 2×4 doorway and aligned window bays → stone weatherproof roof → supported optional details. Existing saved v1 techniques upgrade in place while preserving learned dimensions, successes, and corrections. Inspection now checks completion plus foundation, roof, hollow worker-sized interior, and navigable doorway. The six references/defaults are `cottage/tiny-01`, `longhouse/small-03`, `workshop/small-02`, `townhouse/small-01`, `watchtower/tall-03`, and `hall/large-01`; the original settlement-need ordering, visible-stockpile conservation, persistence, and one-grid worker execution remain unchanged. Grammar tests pass all six families at score 1.0; built bundle `tinyworld-staging-BglhNAtR.js`. Zo Browser opened the debug route but its interaction agent stalled before completing the live button-click check, so final visual/behavioral playtest remains pending.
- **Persistent settlement planner (Jul 13)**: `src/tinyworld-settlement.ts` now adds an inspectable plan above individual building techniques. Five deterministic composition levels generate grouped parcels, shared paths, explicit terrain operations (terrace, cliff stairs, retaining wall, optional cave), landscaping zones (garden, plaza, drainage), dependencies, stages, and inspection state. Workers automatically receive a plan at a terrain-valid anchor, build the next parcel instead of scattering independent structures, report completed projects back to the parcel, and persist/reload the full plan; offline project completion also advances its parcel. `__tw.cityReport()` exposes level/plans and `__tw.settlementStart(level?, workerId?)` creates a fresh plan. Pure planner tests pass all five levels; live bundle `tinyworld-staging-jy0dogXp.js`. Current boundary: grouped parcel execution is live; terrain/landscape cells and dependencies are planned/persisted but their conservation-safe worker execution verbs are not yet wired. See `docs/settlement-planner.md`.

- **Single-generation tree contract (Jul 12)**: `tools/glb_to_world_payload.py` is the sole author of regular trees and stamps `treesUpgraded:true`, `treeUpgradeVersion:3`, plus `treeRecords` in payload metadata. Runtime regular-tree regeneration and the `?retree=1` escape hatch are disabled. Staging may append a super tree only during the first `File` import when `meta.superTree` is absent; it preserves voxelizer regular-tree arrays verbatim, scores the super-tree root court once, and persists `superTree {version, plantedAtMs, cap, origin}`. Saved-world reloads never create, relocate, or rebuild either tree class.

- **Environmental regular-tree voxelization (Jul 12)**: Regular-tree placement is now a deterministic one-time voxelization decision, not a runtime grove replacement. `tools/glb_to_world_payload.py` replaces the old edge-biased six-archetype decorator with `environmental-nyc-v1`: candidate sites are weighted by estimated sky exposure/sunlight, connected soil area, local elevation/drainage, slope/flatness, architecture/canopy clearance, and seeded low-frequency variation; accepted trees apply mature root/canopy spacing so competition suppresses nearby sites without producing a rigid grid. This NYC world uses only the one currently implemented NYC genome, `nyc_honeylocust`; the global Douglas fir, mesquite, and mangrove genomes are not eligible. Branches and asymmetric crowns lean toward open light, crowded/shaded trees become narrower, and roots respond to slope. The payload saves `treeRecords` with species, seed, planted time/age, origin, growth stage, light/moisture/flatness/soil/elevation/competition scores, light+slope directions, mature height, stress, and pruning history. The matching persistent L-system preset and site-response transform live in `src/trees/`; regular trees can therefore derive future age stages from their saved species genome while the super tree remains on its separate space-colonization model. No existing saved world changes until it is regenerated from its original GLB.
- **Procedural-tree voxel cap removed (Jul 12)**: The runtime tree replacement no longer clamps the combined regular/super-tree field to `TREE_BUDGET=40000` or stride-subsamples leaves. That legacy iPhone-oriented cull fragmented the current high-fidelity world's canopy: `w_mrhp6cx5_qvyyj4` had 21,845 saved leaves split into 16,789 6-connected components, versus 27,517 leaves / 3,789 components in the prior world. The generic floater sweep was not responsible; trunk/leaf layers are protected. Staging is currently a web prototype, so procedural trees now retain the generator's complete voxel output. Existing saved worlds keep already-damaged baked leaf data until regenerated from the original GLB; do not use `?retree=1` for that recovery because it also re-clusters/repositions regular trees.
- **Super-tree placement — central gentle rise (Jul 12)**: New/rebuilt super trees now favor a central root court with clear shaft/headroom and a 1–3-voxel rise above the dominant ground, instead of the retired off-center, wall-first selector. Placement records now use `superTree.version: 2`; existing worlds remain untouched on normal load, while an explicit `?retree=1` upgrades an older v1 placement under the new rule.
- **Persistent super-tree life cycle (Jul 12)**: Fresh first/home scans now receive one intentional **root-court** super tree at **50% growth** (its persisted `plantedAtMs` is initialized 6 hours before first build); later new worlds retain the deterministic **12%** rare-tree roll but begin as genuinely young trees. The record is persisted in world metadata as `superTree {version, plantedAtMs, cap, origin}` so reloads preserve both age and the selected off-center, architecture-adjacent root-court origin. The procedural core grows height/canopy and thickens the primary trunk + roots together (with trunk/root emphasis increasing with age). **Important rollback:** automatically migrating already-upgraded ~1M-block saved worlds by re-running the synchronous full tree-field rebuild froze `Building Tiny World`; that migration is disabled on normal load. Existing worlds stay on the fast path until a chunked/background migration exists. Built and restarted after rollback: staging chunk `tinyworld-staging-CAXGoQdW.js`; HTTP 200.
- **Metric GLB uploads route server-side (Jul 10)**: Direct GLB uploads were always voxelized CLIENT-side (worker in `tw-staging-core.tsx`) with scan-era logic — span/345 normalization + rel-height classification. Correct for scale-ambiguous scans; wrong for metric city GLBs (KJ's OSM upload came out 1.568m/voxel, squashed, roofless). Fix: our exporters (`export-osm-glb.ts`, `export-map-glb.ts` — generators `tinyworld-osm-glb`/`tinyworld-map-glb`) are detected in `/api/tinyworld-upload-glb` by sniffing `asset.generator` in the GLB JSON chunk; metric uploads skip ICP-merge nomination and spawn `tools/metric_glb_world.py` (convert() with default `VOXEL_M=0.25` → source-heightfield shell + latent interior rule → `update_world_from_payload`, prior payload backed up). Metric city conversion must not synthesize a flat ceiling/lid layer; roofs come only from source top samples / OSM heightfields, interior mass stays compressed in `latentCols`, and the worn/cratered top pass is opt-in only (`WEAR=1`) because it read as ugly worn ceilings in the prototype. Status tracked in `glb_uploads.merge_status` (`metric_running`/`metric_done`/`metric_error`); the staging client polls and auto-reloads into the corrected world. The client-side voxelization still runs first as a coarse preview — the server overwrite replaces it.
- **0.25m OSM city load budget finding (Jul 10)**: `w_osmcore025_ruins` is not blocked by API transfer size — `/api/tinyworld-worlds?id=w_osmcore025_ruins&blocks=1` returned the 50MB payload locally in ~0.36s. The blocker is client-side synchronous scene construction: the 100m 0.25m core has 2.76M visible voxels, 23.3M latent interior cells, and the ruined wall layer alone logged `1475108 voxels -> 1593070 tris, 1466 chunks`; Chrome pegged the renderer after download. A real JS crash in load cleanup was fixed by making `materializeLatentCell` an early safe function binding before `removeBlockFrom`, then assigning the real implementation later. Generated metric worlds now default-skip decorative triangle grass unless `?grass=1` and skip generic floater sweep unless `?floaters=1`. A DB payload-cropped diagnostic world `w_osmcore025_25m_payloadcrop` was regenerated after removing generated ceiling/lid output and disabling top wear (25m half-crop, 154,457 visible voxels, 1.12M latent cells, layers `dryGrass/dirt/stone/wall` only, no `ceiling` layer, no `wear` metadata), proving the metric/source-roof/latent path can run without the old cap/crater style. Next real fix is a generated-city renderer/serialization fast path (heightfield ground + building shell/column mesh or chunked async meshing), not more payload shrinking.
- **Metric city interiors must be visually solid (Jul 10)**: KJ screenshot showed the prior latent-only technique made buildings read hollow/empty inside. Corrected direction: keep latent interior columns compressed for physics/save/tunneling, but render them as non-collidable vertical prism fill (`layer:"latentFill"`, `metricVisualFill`) in `src/tw-staging-app.tsx`. This gives exposed roofs/cuts solid dead mass without materializing every latent cell as ordinary voxels. Runtime probe on `w_osmcore025_25m_payloadcrop&grass=0&floaters=0&v=latentfill1` found a `latentFill` group with 16,183 column prisms and the world loaded. A fully materialized diagnostic (`w_osmcore025_25m_solid`, 1.25M visible blocks) stalled at worker voxelization/occlusion, confirming ordinary voxel materialization is the wrong scaling technique. The original 100m core still timed out in headless after the prism fix because its wall shell remains too heavy; next optimization should reduce/stream/async the wall shell itself, not remove visual interior fill.
- **OSM extract as open-data city basis (Jul 10)**: `project-tinyworld-voxel-spike/export-osm-glb.ts` converts an OSM XML extract (UES = relation 175905, upload at `/home/.z/chat-uploads/map-dd2e578b75c2.osm`) into a metric GLB on the same ENU frame/anchor as the Google exporter. UES census: 3,080 buildings, 98.6% with height data (NYC DoITT import), 402 building:part ways; landmark towers elsewhere (ESB = 31 stacked parts) keep recognizable setback silhouettes. Faces tessellated to ≤20m edges (voxelizer's 120-step sampler cap ⇒ >30m edges under-sample at 0.5m). Served test asset: `https://kj.zo.space/osm-ues.glb` (170m radius around city anchor).
- **Saved-world tree preservation hotfix (Jul 8)**: KJ reported trees still seemed gone after the floater blocks were cleaned. Data check on `w_mr9wy1eo_a0liwq`: tree data was still present (`3515` trunks / `30165` leaves) and the super-canopy detector still promoted a dominant cluster, but the procedural tree replacement pass was re-running on saved worlds. That pass was designed to convert raw scan trunk/leaf blobs into procedural trees; on an already-saved procedural tree layer it re-detected its own generated trunks as noisy scan input (`145` trunk-column clusters, only `14` height-qualified candidates) and could collapse or move the tree field. Hotfix: procedural tree replacement now runs only for fresh `File` imports or explicit `?retree=1`; normal saved-world loads render the existing saved trunk/leaf voxels as-is.
- **Floater sweep tree-safety hotfix (Jul 8)**: After the self-grounding floater fix, KJ reported the super tree and some trees looked missing. Root cause risk: the new generic floater cleanup was allowed to delete tree/vegetation layers (`trunks`, `leaves`, `fruit`, `seed`, `sapling`) even though legitimate scanned trees can be small disconnected components sitting just above terrain, and super-tree canopy can overhang beyond ground columns. Hotfix in `src/tw-staging-app.tsx`: `FLOAT_SWEEP_PROTECTED_LAYERS` exempts all tree/plant layers from load-time floater deletion; the vegetation sky-ghost code is now report-only (`vegGhostComps`/`vegGhostBlocks`, `vegDeleted` should stay 0). Keep the solid self-grounding fix for dirt/grass/wall/ceiling floaters, but do not re-enable vegetation deletion until there is a trunk/canopy-aware classifier.
- **Phantom surround OFF by default — scan alone in the void (Jul 8, KJ verdict)**: KJ's call after the cloud work: "the clouds are not working — the floating island concept is cleaner and higher quality." Staging default is now **no phantom neighbourhood surround** — the scanned island floats alone in the void (the existing time-of-day sky background + void vignette). Implemented as a default flip, NOT a rebuild: the whole phantom shell (cloud/voxel/photo) was always a pure context layer whose runtime hooks (`phantomSolidAt`/`cloudTick`/`__tilesUpdate`/`__tw.tiles`) start `null` and are only assigned when the surround builds, and every consumer is null-guarded — i.e. the long-supported `?tiles=0` state. New gate near the M2 phantom block in `src/tw-staging-app.tsx` (`_phantomOn`): the surround builds ONLY when explicitly opted in via `?phantom=cloud|voxel|photo` (legacy `?tiles=1` also opts in); `?tiles=0` still force-disables; default (no param) skips it. Everything else about the phantom shell (cloud shader, CloudPack `?cb=1`, scan clearing `?cc=`, voxel/photo modes, align, carve, skirt) is preserved intact behind the opt-in flags for reference/comparison — nothing was deleted. Opt back in: `?phantom=cloud` (soft voxel cloud city), `?phantom=voxel` (dead stone), `?phantom=photo` (streamed photoreal), `?phantom=cloud&cb=1` (CloudPack lumps). Built + restarted; served chain `index-AJSKKHjF.js` → `tinyworld-staging-v_cY0pOy.js`, route 200, `get_space_errors` clean. Headless smoke on `?debugWorld=1` (now light — no phantom to wedge it): `__tw` live, `__tw.tiles` undefined (surround off), no crash. The floating-island **look** is a KJ device call on a real scan world (233K-block worlds wedge headless; the debug world is flat synthetic, not an island). Follow-up offered, not built: a dedicated darker/space "void" backdrop (currently the void is the existing time-of-day sky + vignette, so daytime reads as sky rather than void).
- **Dead-city single-GLB substrate prototype (Jul 9)**: Added opt-in `?phantom=glb` to `src/tw-staging-app.tsx` for the "map-born barren city" experiment: load one explicit GLB into the same phantom/align/void framework as the photo/voxel context layer, without making it scan-owned matter or harvestable ledger material. No Maps GLB file was present in the workspace, so the first seed asset is a placeholder upload `/deadcity-seed.glb` copied from the latest existing TinyWorld GLB; a real Maps export can replace it by uploading as a Space asset and loading with `?phantom=glb&mapglb=/your-map.glb`.
